\left|G(u)-F(\phi_{+})\right|\leq C_{\delta}^{\prime}\big([{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(u)-u\phi_{+}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi_{+})\big)
for all
u\not\in U^{+}_{\delta}\coloneq\left]\rho_{+}-\frac{\delta}{2},\rho_{+}+\frac{\delta}{2}\right[
. The Lipschitz continuity provides the estimate
\left|G(u)-F(\phi_{+})\right|\leq C\delta
for
u\in U_{\delta}^{+}
, so

\int_{E}\left|G(\rho_{n})-F(\phi)\right|\,dx\leq C\left|\Omega\right|\delta+C_{\delta}^{\prime}\int_{E}[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho_{n})-\rho_{n}\phi+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi)\,dx.

The previous paragraph gave
\int_{\Omega}[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho_{n})\,dx\to\int_{\Omega}[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho)\,dx
and
(i)
gives
\rho_{n}\rightharpoonup\rho
weakly in
L^{m}(\Omega)
, so

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\int_{E}\left|G(\rho_{n})-F(\phi)\right|\,dx\leq C\delta+C_{\delta}^{\prime}\int_{E}[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho)-\rho\phi+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi)\,dx=C\delta,

where the last equality follows since
[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho)-\rho\phi+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi)=0
a.e.

A completely analogous argument on the region
\overline{\Omega}\setminus E
is used to control
G(\rho_{n})-F(0)
by
[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho_{n})-\rho_{n}\cdot 0+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(0)
, so combining provides

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left\|G(\rho_{n})-F(\phi)\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C\delta
,
and we send
\delta\to 0^{+}
.

Thus,
\rho_{n}\to\rho
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
, which completes our proof that
\left\{\rho_{n}\right\}
converges strongly along the same subsequence as
\left\{\phi_{n}\right\}
. By interpolating
L^{p}(\Omega)
-norms and using the uniform bound in
L^{m}(\Omega)
, we obtain the strong convergence in
L^{s}(\Omega)
for all
1\leq s<m
, which completes the proof of
(i)
.

Finally, we show
(iv)
. Because
(x,p)\mapsto\left|p\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}
is continuous (a consequence of
(A1)
) as well as positively 1-homogeneous and convex in
p
(consequences of its definition (
2.1
) and
(A2)
), the Reshetnyak lower semicontinuity theorem
[
AFP00
, Theorem 2.38]
provides that the functional

BV(\Omega)\ni\mu\mapsto\int_{\Omega}\left|\frac{\nabla\mu}{\left|\nabla\mu\right|}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d|\nabla\mu|(x)

is lower semicontinuous for weak-
\ast
convergence in
BV(\Omega)
. Above, the notation
\nabla\mu/{\left|\nabla\mu\right|}
denotes the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the measure
\nabla\mu
w.r.t. its total variation
\left|\nabla\mu\right|
. Our work has shown that the auxiliary functions
\left\{\psi_{n}\right\}
converge weakly-
\ast
in
BV(\Omega)
to
\gamma\phi
, so the
vector-valued Radon measures
\left\{\nabla\psi_{n}\right\}
converge weakly-
\ast
to
\nabla(F\circ\phi)=\gamma\nabla\phi
. As a consequence of (
2.9
), (
2.12
), (
3.1
), and Reshetnyak lower semicontinuity, we obtain

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{n},\phi_{n}]\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}]\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\psi_{n}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,dx\geq\gamma\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{\nu}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}d|\nabla\phi|(x)=\mathscr{G}_{0}[\rho,\phi],

which concludes the proof of
(iv)
.
∎

3.3.
Proof of the
\Gamma
-
\liminf
inequality (Theorem
2.1
-
(i)
)

Let
(\rho,\phi)\in L^{1}(\Omega)^{2}
,
\left\{\varepsilon_{n}\right\}
be a positive sequence converging to
0
, and
\left\{(\rho_{n},\phi_{n})\right\}
be a sequence in
L^{1}(\Omega)^{2}
converging in
L^{1}(\Omega)^{2}
to
(\rho,\phi)
. If
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{n},\phi_{n}]=+\infty
, then we are done, so we suppose this limit inferior is finite and pass to a (not relabeled) subsequence that achieves it:
\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{n},\phi_{n}]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{n},\phi_{n}]
. By perhaps passing to a further subsequence, we can suppose
\overline{\mathscr{G}}\coloneq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{n},\phi_{n}]<+\infty
. Now we are in the setting of Proposition
3.6
. By passing to another subsequence, Proposition
3.6
-
(iv)
allows us to conclude.

3.4.
Proof of the
\Gamma
-
\limsup
inequality (Theorem
2.1
-
(ii)
)

The construction of a recovery sequence for
\left\{\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}\right\}
will rely on the construction of a recovery sequence for the Modica-Mortola functionals

\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon}[\phi]\coloneq\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega}W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,\phi)\,dx+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\phi\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}^{2}\,dx&\text{if }\phi\in H^{1}(\Omega),\\
+\infty&\text{otherwise in }L^{1}(\Omega).\end{cases}

We recall
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}[\rho,\phi]\geq\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon}[\phi]
in general (cf. (
2.9
)), and we have equality provided
\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{\Omega}f(\rho)-\rho(\phi-\mathsf{a})+f^{\ast}(\phi-\mathsf{a})\,dx=0
, i.e., when
\rho
is defined pointwise to satisfy (
2.18
). This observation facilitates the construction of a recovery sequence for the
\rho
variable of
\left\{\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}\right\}
from a recovery sequence for
\left\{\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon}\right\}
. In order to satisfy the mass constraint
\int_{\Omega}\rho\,dx=1
on the density, we will also show some stability of our recovery sequences w.r.t. small term-by-term translations of the transition layer.

3.4.1.
A recovery sequence for
\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon}

First, we construct an optimal profile
\omega
for the functionals
\left\{\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon}\right\}
.

Lemma 3.8

(optimal profile)
.

Make the standard assumptions
(SA)
, suppose
f
and
g
are compatible
(F&G)
, and suppose
g
satisfies
(G2)
. Fix
0<\delta<1
and for each
p\in\overline{B_{1}(0)}\setminus B_{\delta}(0)
and
x\in\overline{\Omega}
consider the Cauchy problem

(3.6)

\text{Seek }\omega(\cdot;x,p)\text{ such that }\qquad\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{z}\omega(z;x,p)=-\frac{\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,\omega(z;x,p))}}{\left|p\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}}&\text{for all }z\in\mathbb{R},\\
\displaystyle\omega(0;x,p)=\frac{\phi_{+}}{2}.\end{cases}

(i)

There exists a unique solution
\omega
to (
3.6
). This solution is
C^{1,1}
on
\mathbb{R}\times\overline{\Omega}\times(\overline{B_{1}(0)}\setminus B_{\delta}(0))
and
\omega(\cdot;x,p)
is strictly decreasing.

(ii)

(convergence to
0
) Let
0<\omega_{0}\leq\min\left\{1,a,1/R_{1}\right\}
, where
\mathsf{a}
is from
(F&G)
and
R_{1}
is from
(G2)
. There exists
Z_{0}=Z_{0}(\omega_{0})>0
(large) such that

\forall\,z\geq Z_{0}\qquad 0\leq\omega(z;x,p)\leq\begin{cases}\omega_{0}\exp\left(-\sqrt{\frac{2{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.08888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.08888pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-0.77777pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-0.77777pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}{\overline{{\mathsf{A}}}K_{1}}}(z-Z_{0})\right)&\text{if }q_{0}=2,\\
\omega_{0}\left[1+\omega_{0}^{\frac{q_{0}-2}{2}}(\frac{q_{0}-2}{2})\sqrt{\frac{2{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.08888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.08888pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-0.77777pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-0.77777pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}{\overline{{\mathsf{A}}}K_{1}}}(z-Z_{0})\right]^{-2/(q_{0}-2)}&\text{if }q_{0}>2,\end{cases}

where
q_{0}
is from
(G2)
.

(iii)

(convergence to
\phi_{+}
) If
x\in\Omega_{0}
, then

\forall\,z\leq 0\qquad 0\leq\phi_{+}-\omega(z;x,p)\leq\frac{\phi_{+}}{2}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{2{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,\left\|g^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([\phi_{+}/2,\phi_{+}])}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.33334pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-2.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.66667pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-1.66667pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}z\right).

Proof.

(i)
: We claim
v\mapsto\sqrt{W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)}/\left|p\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}
is Lipschitz in
[0,\phi_{+}]
uniformly in
(x,p)\in\overline{\Omega}\times(\overline{B_{1}(0)}\setminus B_{\delta}(0))
, so the existence and uniqueness of
\omega(\cdot;x,p)
will follow from Cauchy-Lipschitz. Since
W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,\cdot)
is continuously differentiable (Lemma
3.3
-
(i)
), we only need to address its growth near its zeros, i.e.,
v=0
(if
x\in\overline{\Omega}
) and
v=\phi_{+}
(if
x\in\Omega_{0}
). By definition (
2.11
),

W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)={W_{\ast}}(v)+(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(v).

If
x\in\Omega_{0}
, then
W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)={W_{\ast}}(v)
, so for the growth near
v=0
, Lemma
3.2
-
(vi)
gives

\forall\,\left|v\right|\leq\min\left\{1,a,1/R_{1}\right\}\qquad\left|\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)}-\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,0)}\right|\leq\sqrt{2{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,K_{1}}\left|v\right|^{q_{1}/2}.

Since
q_{1}/2\geq(1+(1/2)q_{0})/2\geq 1
(recall from
(G2)
that
q_{0}\geq 2
) and
\left|v\right|\leq 1
, we have
\left|v\right|^{q_{1}/2}\leq\left|v\right|
. Otherwise, for
x\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{0}
, the double-well potential is perturbed
W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)={W_{\ast}}(v)+(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(v)
, so we combine Lemma
3.2
-
(vi)
with
(G2)
to obtain for these small
v

\left|\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)}-\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,0)}\right|\leq\sqrt{2\mathsf{c}(x)K_{1}}\left|v\right|^{q_{1}/2}\leq\sqrt{2\max_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}\mathsf{c}(x)K_{1}}\left|v\right|.

If
x\in\Omega_{0}
, then
W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)={W_{\ast}}(v)
, so the growth near
v=\phi_{+}
is described by Lemma
3.2
-
(vii)
and we can conclude.

It is straightforward, in view of
(A1)
and
(C1)
, to check that

\sqrt{2\big(\mathsf{c}(x)g(v)-f^{\ast}(v-\mathsf{a})\big)}/\left|\sqrt{p\cdot{\mathsf{A}}(x)p}\right|

depends in a continuously differentiable manner on the parameters
(x,p)\in\overline{\Omega}\times(\overline{B_{1}(0)}\setminus B_{\delta}(0))
.

(ii)
: Since
W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)={W_{\ast}}(v)+(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(v)\geq{W_{\ast}}(v)
, we estimate

\partial_{z}\omega=-\frac{\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,\omega)}}{\sqrt{p\cdot{\mathsf{A}}(x)p}}\leq-\frac{\sqrt{2{W_{\ast}}(\omega)}}{\sqrt{\overline{{\mathsf{A}}}}}.

Since
\omega(\cdot;x,p)
is monotone decreasing, the above bound shows there exists
Z_{0}>0
, independent of
x,p
, large enough such that for all
z\geq Z_{0}
we have
\omega(z;x,p)\leq\omega_{0}\leq\min\left\{1,a,1/R_{1}\right\}
. For these large
z
, Lemma
3.2
-
(vi)
gives
{W_{\ast}}(\omega)\geq{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,\left|\omega\right|^{q_{0}}/K_{1}
, so
\partial_{z}\omega\leq-\sqrt{\frac{2{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.08888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.08888pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-0.77777pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-0.77777pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}{\overline{{\mathsf{A}}}K_{1}}}\omega^{q_{0}/2}
, and the remainder of the proof is elementary.

(iii)
: For
x\in\Omega_{0}
, we have
W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)={W_{\ast}}(v)
, which combined with Lemma
3.2
-
(vii)
gives

\partial_{z}(\phi_{+}-\omega)=\frac{\sqrt{2{W_{\ast}}(\omega)}}{\sqrt{p\cdot{\mathsf{A}}(x)p}}\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{2{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,\left\|g^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([\phi_{+}/2,\phi_{+}])}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.33334pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-2.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.66667pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-1.66667pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}(\phi_{+}-\omega).

We dropped the absolute value since
\omega(z)<\phi_{+}
for all
z
. Gronwall’s lemma gives the estimate.
∎

We first construct a recovery sequence for
\left\{\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon}\right\}
when
\phi=\phi_{+}\chi_{E}
with
E
having
C^{2}
boundary.

Lemma 3.9
.

Suppose
(SA)
,
(F&G)
,
(C1)
, and
(G2)
. Let
\left\{\varepsilon_{n}\right\}
be a positive sequence converging to zero and
\phi=\phi_{+}\chi_{E}\in BV(\Omega;\left\{0,\phi_{+}\right\})
satisfy
\int_{\Omega}\phi\,dx=\phi_{+}/\rho_{+}
and
\phi\chi_{\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{0}}=0
a.e. If
E
is open and has
C^{2}
boundary, then there exists a sequence
\left\{\phi_{n}\right\}\subset H^{1}(\Omega)
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi_{n}=\phi\text{ in }L^{1}(\Omega)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}]\leq\gamma\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{\nu}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d|\nabla\phi|(x),

where
\gamma
is the constant defined by (
2.14
) and
\vec{\nu}
is the outer unit normal vector to
\partial E
.

Proof.

The complementarity condition
\phi\chi_{\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{0}}=0
implies
\overline{E}\subset\Omega_{0}
. We will denote by
d_{\partial E}
the signed distance function on
\overline{\Omega}
to
\partial E
with the sign convention that
\left\{d_{\partial E}\leq 0\right\}=\overline{E}
.
We introduce

(3.7)

\Phi_{n}(z;x)\coloneq\begin{cases}\phi_{+}&\text{if }z<-2,\\
\phi_{+}-\Big(\phi_{+}-\omega\big(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}};x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\big)\Big)\big(2+z\big)&\text{if }-2\leq z<-1,\\
\omega\big(\frac{z}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}};x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\big)&\text{if }-1\leq z<1,\\
\omega\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}};x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\big)\big(2-z\big)&\text{if }1\leq z<2,\\
0&\text{if }z\geq 2,\end{cases}

where
\omega(\cdot;x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x))
is the optimal profile from Lemma
3.8
.

We show the sequence
\left\{\phi_{n}\right\}
defined by

\phi_{n}(x)\coloneq\Phi_{n}\big(\frac{d_{\partial E}(x)}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}};x\big)

has the desired properties.

We recall some basic facts about the (signed) distance function; see, e.g.,
[
DZ11
, Chapters 6 and 7]
. The signed distance function is Lipschitz, so it is differentiable a.e. by Rademacher. Also, at points
x\in\Omega
where
d_{\partial E}
is differentiable we have
\left|\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\right|=1
, which is compatible with the cut-off scale
0<\delta<1
for the parameter
p\in\overline{B_{1}(0)}\setminus B_{\delta}(0)
in the definition of
\omega
. In fact, for
\varepsilon_{n}
sufficiently small,
d_{\partial E}\in C^{2}(\left\{\left|d_{\partial E}\right|<3\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\})
because
\partial E
is
C^{2}
. Together, for large
n
, these imply
\phi_{n}\in W^{1,\infty}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow H^{1}(\Omega)
, with the embedding following since
\Omega
is bounded. Using
0<\omega<\phi_{+}
, it is straightforward to verify that
\phi_{n}\to\phi
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
.

We split the integral:

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,\phi_{n}(x))+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}\left|\nabla\phi_{n}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}^{2}\,dx=\int_{\left\{\left|d_{\partial E}\right|/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq 1\right\}}+\int_{\left\{1\leq\left|d_{\partial E}\right|/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq 2\right\}}+\int_{\left\{\left|d_{\partial E}\right|/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}>2\right\}}.

The last integral is zero since
\phi_{n}
is constant on each of
\left\{d_{\partial E}/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}<-2\right\}
and
\left\{d_{\partial E}/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\geq 2\right\}
, which causes the interface penalty in
\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}
to vanish. Because on this region
\phi_{n}
is, in particular,
0
or
\phi_{+}
, i.e., one of the well locations, the integral of
W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(\cdot,\phi_{n})
vanishes.

On the right-hand side, the second integral is negligible as
n\to\infty
. We sketch the argument for the region
\left\{1\leq d_{\partial E}/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}<2\right\}
, where the rate of convergence of the profile
\omega
to
0
may not be exponential (Lemma
3.8
-
(ii)
).
We start with the contribution from
W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(\cdot,\phi_{n})
. Let
Z_{0}>0
be from Lemma
3.8
-
(ii)
and
N
be so large that
\varepsilon_{n}<1/Z_{0}^{2}
for all
n\geq N
, so Lemma
3.8
-
(ii)
gives for any
q_{0}\geq 2

\text{for all }n\geq N\text{ and a.e.\ }x\in\left\{1\leq d_{\partial E}/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}<2\right\}\qquad 0<\phi_{n}(x)\leq\omega_{0}\leq\min\left\{1,a,1/R_{1}\right\}.

Then Lemma
3.2
-
(vi)
and
(G2)
together give for these
x
and large
n

\displaystyle W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,\phi_{n}(x))={W_{\ast}}(\phi_{n}(x))+(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(\phi_{n}(x))\leq\big(\max_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}\mathsf{c}(x)\big)K_{1}\phi_{n}(x)^{q_{1}}\lesssim\left[c+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}\right]^{-\frac{2q_{1}}{q_{0}-2}},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
3.8
-
(ii)
in the pessimistic case of
q_{0}>2
and
c>0
is a constant independent of
x
and
n
. Consequently,

\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\left\{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq d_{\partial E}<2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}}W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,\phi_{n}(x))\,dx\lesssim\left[c\varepsilon_{n}^{\frac{q_{0}-2}{2q_{1}}}+\varepsilon_{n}^{\frac{q_{0}-2-q_{1}}{2q_{1}}}\right]^{-\frac{2q_{1}}{q_{0}-2}}\left|\left\{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq d_{\partial E}<2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}\right|,

and since the measure of this region is
O(\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}})
it is straightforward to check that the right-hand side vanishes as
n\to\infty
under the condition
1+\frac{q_{0}}{2}\leq q_{1}\leq q_{0}
from
(G2)
. For the contribution from the interface penalty, we have
\varepsilon_{n}\nabla\phi_{n}(x)\cdot{\mathsf{A}}(x)\nabla\phi_{n}(x)=O(\varepsilon_{n})
for
x\in\left\{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq d_{\partial E}<2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
. After integration over
\left\{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\leq d_{\partial E}<2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
, its contribution to
\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}]
is
O(\varepsilon_{n}^{3/2})
and hence negligible as
n\to\infty
, too.

We examine the region near the interface:
\left\{\left|d_{\partial E}\right|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
. Here, the density of the interface penalty is, by the Cauchy problem (
3.6
) satisfied by
\omega
,

\nabla\phi_{n}(x)\cdot{\mathsf{A}}(x)\nabla\phi_{n}(x)=\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}}W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}\big(x,\omega\big(\frac{d_{\partial E}(x)}{\varepsilon_{n}};x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\big)\big)+O(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}).

The last term is negligible after multiplication by
\varepsilon_{n}
since it is integrated over a domain of measure
O(\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}})
. Overall,

\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}]=\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\left\{\left|d_{\partial E}\right|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}}W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}\big(x,\omega\big(\frac{d_{\partial E}(x)}{\varepsilon_{n}};x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\big)\big)\,dx+o_{n\to\infty}(1).

As is standard, the remaining integral is computed by the coarea formula
[
AFP00
, Theorem 2.93]
. We make two changes of variable. First, we let
z\coloneq\lambda/\varepsilon_{n}
, so

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\left\{\left|d_{\partial E}\right|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}}W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}\big(x,\omega\big(\frac{d_{\partial E}(x)}{\varepsilon_{n}};x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\big)\big)\left|\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\right|\,dx

\displaystyle=2\int_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}}^{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}}\int_{\left\{d_{\partial E}=\varepsilon_{n}z\right\}}W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,\omega(z;x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)))\,d\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x)\,dz,

Consequently,

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}]\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}2\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{\left\{d_{\partial E}=\varepsilon_{n}z\right\}}W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,\omega(z;x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)))\,d\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x)\,dz.

Second, Lemma
3.8
-
(i)
gives for each
x
that the optimal profile
z\mapsto\omega(z;x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x))
is monotone (decreasing), so we change variables from signed distance to concentration
v\coloneq\omega(z;x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x))
. Then, (
3.6
) provides
dv=\partial_{z}\omega(\cdot;x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x))\,dz=-\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)}/\left|\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,dz
. Since

\lim_{z\to+\infty}\omega(z)=0
(Lemma
3.8
-
(ii)
) and

\lim_{z\to-\infty}\omega(z;x,\nabla d_{\partial E}(x))=\phi_{+}
for
x\in\left\{d_{\partial E}\leq 0\right\}\subset\Omega_{0}
(Lemma
3.8
-
(iii)
),
this change of variable yields

\displaystyle=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\phi_{+}}\int_{\left\{\omega(d_{\partial E}(\cdot)/\varepsilon_{n};\cdot,\nabla d_{\partial E}(\cdot))=v\right\}}\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)}\left|\nabla d_{\partial E}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x)\,dv.

The remainder of the proof consists of computing the above limit superior. By computations analogous to those in
[
OS91
, Section 3]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}]\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\phi_{+}}\int_{\left\{\omega(d_{\partial E}(\cdot)/\varepsilon_{n};\cdot,\nabla d_{\partial E}(\cdot))=v\right\}}\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)}\left|\nabla d_{\partial E}\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x)\,dv

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\phi_{+}}\int_{\partial E}\sqrt{2W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)}\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d\mathcal{H}^{d-1}(x)\,dv=\gamma\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d|\nabla\phi|(x).

The last equality follows from recalling
\left|\nabla\phi\right|=\phi_{+}\mathcal{H}^{d-1}\lfloor\partial E
and
\partial E\subset\Omega_{0}
, the latter of which yields
W_{\ast\mathsf{c}}(x,v)={W_{\ast}}(v)
.
∎

3.4.2.
Stability of the recovery sequence

We recall that
\rho
, and not
\phi
, in
\left\{\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}\right\}
satisfies a mass constraint. The recovery sequences we constructed for
\left\{\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon}\right\}
are stable to small term-by-term translations of the location of the transition layer in the profile
\Phi_{n}
(cf. (
3.7
)) away or towards
\partial E
.

Lemma 3.10

(stability)
.

Suppose
(SA)
,
(F&G)
,
(C1)
, and
(G2)
. Let
\left\{\varepsilon_{n}\right\}
be a positive sequence converging to zero,
E\subset\overline{\Omega}
open with
C^{2}
boundary, and
\phi\coloneq\phi_{+}\chi_{E}
satisfy
\int_{\Omega}\phi\,dx=\phi_{+}/\rho_{+}
and
\phi\chi_{\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{0}}=0

\mathcal{L}^{d}
-a.e. Let
\left\{\phi_{n}\right\}
be the sequence
\phi_{n}(x)\coloneq\Phi_{n}(\frac{d_{\partial E}(x)}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}};x)
constructed in Lemma
3.9
, with
\Phi_{n}
defined in (
3.7
). For any sequence
\left\{\tau_{n}\right\}\subset[-2,2]
,
the sequence
\left\{\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}\right\}
defined by
\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}(x)\coloneq\Phi_{n}(\frac{d_{\partial E}(x)}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n};x)
satisfies

\text{for }n\text{ large }\left\|\phi^{\tau_{n}}_{n}-\phi_{n}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}=O(\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\left|\tau_{n}\right|),\quad\text{and}\quad\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi^{\tau_{n}}_{n}]\leq\gamma\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{\nu}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d|\nabla\phi(x)|.

Proof.

By taking
n
large enough, we may assume
\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\left|\tau_{n}\right|<1/2
. Suppose
\tau_{n}>0
, so
\phi^{\tau_{n}}_{n}\geq\phi_{n}
for all
x
since, for fixed
x
, the profile
z\mapsto\Phi_{n}(z;x)
is nonincreasing (cf. (
3.7
)). We sketch why the
L^{1}
-distance between
\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}
and
\phi_{n}
is
O(\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})
; the case
\tau_{n}<0
is analogous.

For a fixed
x
(that we suppress),

(3.8)

\footnotesize\begin{gathered}0\leq\Phi_{n}(z-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})-\Phi_{n}(z)\\
=\begin{cases}\big(\phi_{+}-\omega(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})\big)(2+z)=O(e^{-1/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})&\text{if }-2\leq z<-2+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n},\\
\big(\phi_{+}-\omega(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})\big)\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau=O(e^{-1/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})&\text{if }-2+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}\leq z<-1,\\
\phi_{+}-(\phi_{+}-\omega(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}))(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}+z)-\omega(\frac{z}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})=O\big(e^{-1/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}(e^{\tau_{n}}-1)\big)&\text{if }-1\leq z<-1+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n},\\
\omega(\frac{z-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})-\omega(\frac{z}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})=O(1)&\text{if }-1+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}\leq z<1,\\
\omega(\frac{z-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})-\omega(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})(2-z)=O(\varepsilon_{n}^{q_{1}/(2(q_{0}-2))}\tau_{n})&\text{if }1\leq z<1+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n},\\
\omega(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}=O(\varepsilon_{n}^{1/(q_{0}-2)}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})&\text{if }1+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}\leq z<2,\\
\omega(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})(2+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}-z)=O(\varepsilon_{n}^{1/(q_{0}-2)}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})&\text{if }2\leq z<2+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}.\end{cases}\end{gathered}

We sketch how to estimate the difference for each of the cases above. For the first two cases, both profiles are close to
\phi_{+}
while in the last two cases both profiles are close to
0
, so our estimates on the rate of convergence of
\omega
provide that the profile and its translation are, respectively, exponentially close to one another (Lemma
3.8
-
(iii)
) or (at worst) algebraically close to one another (Lemma
3.8
-
(ii)
). For the third and fifth cases, the estimates are more delicate, but essentially the same conclusion holds. In the fourth case, where the profiles are simultaneously near their transition layers, the integral of the difference is
O(\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})
after changing to the macroscopic variable
z\coloneq d_{\partial E}(x)/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}
.

We show how to handle the fifth case, which is the most delicate. Let
Z_{0}
be from Lemma
3.8
-
(ii)
, and by taking
n
sufficiently large, we may suppose
1/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\geq 2Z_{0}
, which gives us access to the rate of convergence of
\omega
to
0
(Lemma
3.8
-
(ii)
).

Since
z-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}\geq 1-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}
, the profile
z\mapsto\omega(z)
is nonincreasing, and
2-z>1-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau
,

\omega(\frac{z-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})-\omega(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})(2-z)\leq\omega(\frac{1-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})-\omega(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}\omega(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}).

The last term is (at worst)
O(\varepsilon_{n}^{1/(q_{0}-2)}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})
. We estimate the difference using the Cauchy problem for
\omega
. Because
\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}<1/2
and
1/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\geq 2Z_{0}
, we have
(1-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}>Z_{0}
, so the rate of convergence of
\omega
to
0
gives for
\zeta\geq(1-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}
that
\omega(\zeta)\leq\omega_{0}\leq\min\left\{1,a,1/R_{1}\right\}
. Then the growth of
{W_{\ast}}
near
0
(Lemma
3.2
-
(vi)
) shows
\sqrt{{W_{\ast}}(\omega(\zeta))}\lesssim\omega(\zeta)^{q_{1}/2}
, so the rate of convergence to
0
provides

\displaystyle\omega(\frac{1-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})-\omega(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})\lesssim\int_{(1-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}^{1/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}\sqrt{{W_{\ast}}(\omega(\zeta))}\,d\zeta\lesssim\int_{(1-\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}^{1/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}(c+\zeta)^{-\frac{q_{1}}{q_{0}-2}}\,d\zeta

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{n}(c+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}-\tau_{n})^{-q_{1}/(q_{0}-2)}\leq\tau_{n}(c+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}})^{-q_{1}/(q_{0}-2)}.

The constant
c>0
arises from Lemma
3.8
-
(ii)
; and is uniform over
\zeta
and large
n
.

Combining the pointwise estimates we derived for the first three or last three regions in (
3.8
) with an analogous calculation using the coarea formula shows the differences
\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}-\phi_{n}
in
L^{1}
are
O(\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}\tau_{n})
. Since
\phi_{n}\to\phi
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
, we thus deduce
\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}\to\phi
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
, too.
∎

3.4.3.
A recovery sequence for
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}

Let
\left\{\varepsilon_{n}\right\}
be a positive sequence converging to zero and
(\rho,\phi)\in L^{1}(\Omega)^{2}
. If
\mathscr{G}_{0}[\rho,\phi]=+\infty
, then we may arbitrarily choose any sequence
\left\{(\rho_{n},\phi_{n})\right\}\subset L^{1}(\Omega)^{2}
converging to
(\rho,\phi)
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
and be done. Now, suppose
\mathscr{G}_{0}[\rho,\phi]<+\infty
, so
\rho\in BV(\Omega;\left\{0,\rho_{+}\right\})
and
\phi\in BV(\Omega;\left\{0,\phi_{+}\right\})
and they satisfy
\rho=\frac{\rho_{+}}{\phi_{+}}\phi
a.e.,
\phi\chi_{\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{0}}=0
a.e., and
\int_{\Omega}\rho\,dx=1
. Then,
\phi=\phi_{+}\chi_{E}
for some set of finite perimeter
E
with
\left|E\right|=1/\rho_{+}
. We further assume
E
is open with
C^{2}
boundary. We relax this later.

Consider a recovery sequence
\left\{\phi_{n}\right\}
for
\phi
and the functionals
\left\{\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
, let
\left\{\tau_{n}\right\}\subset[-2,2]
be a sequence of translations that is to be determined, and let
\left\{\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}\right\}
be the term-by-term translations of
\left\{\phi_{n}\right\}
as in Lemma
3.10
. In particular,
\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}\to\phi
in
L^{1}(\Omega)
. Since
f^{\ast}
is continuously differentiable, we define

(3.9)

\text{a.e.\ }x\in\Omega\qquad\rho_{n}(x)\coloneq{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}(\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}(x)-\mathsf{a}),

where
\mathsf{a}
is the constant from
(F&G)
.

Each
\rho_{n}
is nonnegative as a consequence of the nonnegativity of
{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}
. To see the latter, note
{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}
is nondecreasing (as the derivative of a convex function), and because
f^{\ast}\equiv 0
on
\left]-\infty,0\right]
(since
f\equiv+\infty
there) we have
{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}\equiv 0
on
\left]-\infty,0\right]
, too.

We now choose the translations
\left\{\tau_{n}\right\}
via a continuity argument so as to enforce the mass constraint
\int_{\Omega}\rho_{n}\,dx=1
. For a fixed
n
, we note if
\tau_{n}=-2
then the translation satisfies
\phi_{n}^{-2}\leq\phi
a.e. Because
{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}
is nondecreasing, we deduce
{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}(\phi_{n}^{-2}-\mathsf{a})\leq{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}(\phi-\mathsf{a})=\rho
a.e.

\int_{\Omega}{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}(\phi_{n}^{-2}-\mathsf{a})\,dx\leq\int_{\Omega}{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}(\phi-\mathsf{a})\,dx=\int\rho\,dx=1.

If
\tau_{n}=2
, then
\phi_{n}^{2}\geq\phi
a.e., so analogous reasoning shows

\int_{\Omega}{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}(\phi_{n}^{2}-\mathsf{a})\,dx\geq\int_{\Omega}{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}(\phi-\mathsf{a})\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\rho\,dx=1.

For large enough
n
, the map
[-2,2]\ni\tau\mapsto\phi_{n}^{\tau}\in L^{1}(\Omega)
is Lipschitz and nondecreasing, and the monotonicity and continuity of
{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}
allow to show
[-2,2]\ni\tau\mapsto\int_{\Omega}{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}(\phi_{n}^{\tau}-\mathsf{a})\,dx\in[1-\delta,1+\delta]
is continuous. Intermediate value theorem gives
\tau(\varepsilon_{n})\in[-2,2]
such that
\int_{\Omega}{f^{\ast}}^{\prime}(\phi_{n}^{\tau(\varepsilon_{n})}-\mathsf{a})\,dx=1
, so we choose
\tau_{n}\coloneq\tau(\varepsilon_{n})
.

We show the
\Gamma
-
\limsup
inequality. Suppose
n
is large enough that the mass constraint
\int_{\Omega}\rho_{n}\,dx=1
is satisfied and thus
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{n},\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}]<+\infty
. We appeal to the formula (
2.8
) for
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
:

\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{n},\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}]=\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}]+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}f(\rho_{n})-\rho_{n}(\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}-\mathsf{a})+f^{\ast}(\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}-\mathsf{a})\,dx.

Our definition of
\left\{\rho_{n}\right\}
in (
3.9
) was chosen such that the integrand on the right-hand side is zero a.e. Consequently,
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{n},\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}]=\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}]
, so by using that
\left\{\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}\right\}
is a recovery sequence for
\left\{\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
(Lemma
3.10
), we deduce

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{n},\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}]=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{F}_{\mathsf{c},\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{n}^{\tau_{n}}]\leq\mathscr{G}_{0}[\rho,\phi].

By Remark
3.7
,

\rho_{n}\to\rho
in
L^{1}
since, after finitely many terms,
\left\{(\rho_{n},\phi_{n})\right\}

(WP)
and
\phi_{n}\to\phi
in
L^{1}
.

We show there was no loss of generality to assume
E
was open with
C^{2}
boundary. Suppose
E\subset\Omega_{0}
is a set of finite perimeter in
\Omega
. Because
\Omega,\Omega_{0}
have Lipschitz boundaries, there exists
[
Ste88
, Lemma 1]
,
[
AFP00
, Remark 3.43]
a sequence
\left\{E_{j}\right\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
of open sets with smooth boundary in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
we have
\left|E_{j}\cap\Omega_{0}\right|=\left|E\right|
and

\lim_{j\to\infty}\left|(E_{j}\cap\Omega_{0})\Delta E\right|=0
and
\lim_{j\to\infty}\Per(E_{j},\Omega)=\Per(E,\Omega).

Set
\phi_{j}\coloneq\phi_{+}\chi_{E_{j}}
and
\rho_{j}\coloneq(\rho_{+}/\phi_{+})\phi_{j}
, so the above means each of
\left\{\rho_{j}\right\}
,
\left\{\phi_{j}\right\}
converge strictly in
BV(\Omega)
to
\rho,\phi
, resp. We then apply our previous work term-by-term to
\left\{(\rho_{j},\phi_{j})\right\}
and obtain for each
j
a sequence
\left\{(\rho_{j,n},\phi_{j,n})\right\}_{n}
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}(\rho_{j,n},\phi_{j,n})=(\rho_{j},\phi_{j})\text{ in }L^{1}(\Omega)^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{j,n},\phi_{j,n}]\leq\gamma\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{\nu}_{j}\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d|\nabla\phi_{j}|\eqcolon\mathscr{G}_{0}[\rho_{j},\phi_{j}].

The Reshetnyak continuity theorem gives

\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathscr{G}_{0}[\rho_{j},\phi_{j}]=\mathscr{G}_{0}[\rho,\phi]
, so

\limsup_{j\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{j,n},\phi_{j,n}]\leq\mathscr{G}_{0}[\rho,\phi]
.
A diagonalization argument gives the conclusion.

4.
The limiting continuity equation and phase separation

The aim of this section is twofold. First, by viewing the first equation in (

P
ε

) as a continuity equation with density
\rho_{\varepsilon}
and velocity
\vec{v}_{\varepsilon}
, we show a subsequence of these solutions converge as
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
to a distributional solution
(\rho,\vec{v})
of the continuity equation (Proposition
4.2
). Second, we show perhaps along a further subsequence that the chemoattractant becomes phase separated (Proposition
4.4
), and along the same subsequence the density converges to a scalar multiple of limiting chemoattractant, and thus also undergoes phase separation. This section complete the proofs of Theorem
2.2
-
(ii)
,
(i)
. We emphasize that this section does
not
make use of the energy convergence hypothesis
(EC)
.

4.1.
Convergence of the continuity equation (Proof of Theorem
2.2
-
(ii)
)

We derive, using the energy dissipation inequality, well-preparedness of the initial data (
(WP)
), and coercivity, some classical a priori estimates for gradient flows and distributional solutions of the continuity equation. The convergence of a subsequence of
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\vec{v}_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\}
is obtained by exhibiting some precompactness for the family of densities and the family of velocities. These are essentially those in
[
Mel24
, Lemma 4.1]
.

Lemma 4.1
.

If
(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})
solves (

P
ε

) with well-prepared
(WP)
initial data
(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}})
, then

(i)

for all
t\in[0,T]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\leq\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\leq{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}
and
\int_{0}^{t}\mathscr{D}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(s)\,ds\leq{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}
;

(ii)

denoting
\vec{j}_{\varepsilon}\coloneq\rho_{\varepsilon}\vec{v}_{\varepsilon}
, we have
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\leq{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}
;

(iii)

there exists a constant
C({\overline{\mathscr{G}}})>0
such that

(4.1)

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\rho\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{m}(\Omega))}<C({\overline{\mathscr{G}}}),\qquad\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{2m}{m+1}}(\Omega))}<C({\overline{\mathscr{G}}}),

(4.2)

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(s)\right\|_{(W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega))^{\ast}}\leq C({\overline{\mathscr{G}}})\left|t-s\right|^{1/2}\quad\forall\,t,s\in[0,T].

Proof.

(i)
: The first inequality is a direct consequence of the definition of
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}
(recall (
2.8
) and (
2.12
)). The second inequality follows from the energy dissipation inequality (
2.19
), the nonnegativity of the dissipation term
\int_{0}^{t}\mathscr{D}_{\varepsilon}(s)\,ds
, and the well-preparedness of the initial data. The bound on the dissipation term follows again by the energy dissipation inequality and the bound on
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}
.

(ii)
: Rewriting the dissipation from the continuity equation using the mass flux
\vec{j}_{\varepsilon}\coloneq\rho_{\varepsilon}\vec{v}_{\varepsilon}
gives

\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\!\!\int_{\Omega}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\left|\vec{v}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt=\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\!\!\int_{\Omega}\frac{\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|^{2}}{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}}\,dx\,dt.

Then Cauchy-Schwarz and that
\rho_{\varepsilon}(t)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{ac}}(\Omega)
for all
t\geq 0
give

\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|\,dx\leq\left(\int_{\Omega}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\,dx\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|^{2}}{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)}\,dx\right)^{1/2}=\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|^{2}}{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)}\,dx\right)^{1/2},

so squaring, integrating over
t\in[0,T]
, and the energy dissipation inequality (
2.19
) provide

\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}\,dt\leq\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\!\!\int_{\Omega}\frac{\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|^{2}}{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}}\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathscr{D}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\,dt\leq\overline{\mathscr{G}}.

(iii)
: For the bound on the density, fix
t\in[0,T]
and then
(i)
shows the sequence
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]
is bounded uniformly-in
t
-and-
n
, so Lemma
3.5
-
(ii)
gives

\left\|\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right\|_{L^{m}(\Omega)}\leq C(\overline{\mathscr{G}}\varepsilon_{n})^{1/m}+C\qquad\text{for all }\,n\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }t\in[0,T].

Since
\left\{\varepsilon_{n}\right\}_{n}
is bounded and the upper bound is independent of
t
, the estimate follows.

For the bound on the mass flux, fix
t\geq 0
, so Hölder’s inequality with
p=m+1
and
p^{\prime}=\frac{m+1}{m}
gives

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|^{2/p^{\prime}}\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\left|\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|^{1/p^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|^{2}}{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)}\right)^{1/p^{\prime}}\,dx\leq\left(\int_{\Omega}\left|\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|^{m}\,dx\right)^{1/p}\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|^{2}}{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)}\,dx\right)^{1/p^{\prime}},

and then the uniform
L^{\infty}_{t}L^{m}_{x}
-bound on the density gives

\left\|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right\|_{L^{\frac{2m}{m+1}}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq C\int_{\Omega}\frac{\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|^{2}}{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)}\,dx.

Integration over
t\in[0,T]
and the energy dissipation inequality (
2.19
) provide the estimate.

For the uniform equicontinuity (
4.2
), we first note that by density of
C^{1}(\overline{\Omega})
in
W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega)
and linearity, the weak formulation of the continuity equation is satisfied for test functions in
C^{1}_{c}(\left[0,T\right[;W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega))
. Moreover, since
(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\vec{v}_{\varepsilon_{n}})
satisfy the weak formulation on the time interval
[0,T]
, they also satisfy it over the time intervals
[0,s]\subset[0,t]\subset[0,T]
. Thus, given a test function
\zeta\in W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega)
, we take the difference of the weak formulations over the time intervals
[0,t]
and
[0,s]

\int_{\Omega}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(s))\zeta\,dx=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\vec{v}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\nabla\zeta\,dx\,d\tau.

Then Hölder’s inequality with
p=\frac{2m}{m+1}
and
p^{\prime}=\frac{2m}{m-1}
and the uniform
L^{2}_{t}L^{\frac{2m}{m+1}}_{x}
-bound on the flux gives

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(s))\zeta\,dx\right|\leq\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|\left|\nabla\zeta\right|\,dx\,d\tau\leq\left(\int_{s}^{t}\left\|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\tau)\right\|_{L^{\frac{2m}{m+1}}_{x}}\,d\tau\right)\left\|\nabla\zeta\right\|_{L^{\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\sqrt{t-s}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{2m}{m+1}}(\Omega))}\big)\left\|\zeta\right\|_{W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega)},

which implies the estimate.
∎

We show there is a limit point
(\rho,\vec{j})
of
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\}
that solves the continuity equation.

Proposition 4.2

(limiting continuity equation)
.

Assume the hypotheses of the previous lemma.

(i)

(compactness) There exists a (not relabeled) subsequence
\left\{\varepsilon_{n}\right\}
,

(4.3)

\begin{gathered}\rho\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{m}(\Omega))\cap C([0,T];(W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega))^{\ast})\text{ and }\vec{j}\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{2m}{m+1}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))\end{gathered}

such that
\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\to\rho
strongly in
C([0,T];(W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega))^{\ast})
and weakly-
\ast
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{m}(\Omega))
, and
\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\rightharpoonup\vec{j}
weakly in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{2m}{m+1}}(\Omega)^{d})
.

(ii)

There exists
\vec{v}\in L^{2}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega,\rho\,dxdt;\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\vec{j}=\rho\vec{v}
a.e. in
\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega
and the pair
(\rho,\vec{v})
satisfies the distributional formulation of the continuity equation (
2.21
).

Proof.

(i): The weak convergences follows immediately from the uniform bounds in Lemma
4.1
-
(iii)
. The strong convergence is a consequence of Arzelà-Ascoli. Indeed, since we work on a bounded domain, we have the compact embedding
W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow L^{\frac{m}{m-1}}(\Omega)
, so Schauder provides the compact embedding
L^{m}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow(W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega))^{\ast}
, and then the uniform
L^{\infty}_{t}L^{m}_{x}
-bound on
\left\{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
implies the sequence is bounded in
C([0,T];(W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega))^{\ast})
and for each
t
that
\left\{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right\}_{n}
is precompact in
(W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega))^{\ast}
. The requisite uniform equicontinuity also from Lemma
4.1
-
(iii)
allows us to conclude.

(ii): After replacing
\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\vec{v}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
with
\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
, the distributional formulation of the continuity equation reads

\int_{\Omega}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}}\zeta(0)\,dx+\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\!\!\int_{\Omega}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\partial_{t}\zeta\,dx\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\!\!\int_{\Omega}\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\nabla\zeta\,dx\,d\tau=0\qquad\forall\,\zeta\in C^{1}_{c}(\left[0,T\right[\times\overline{\Omega}).

The strong convergence of
\left\{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
in
C([0,T];(W^{1,\frac{2m}{m-1}}(\Omega))^{\ast})
permits us to pass to the limit in the first term, and the fact that the limit is in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{m}(\Omega))
allows us to represent this term again as an integral. The weak convergences from (i) allow to pass to the limit in the second and third terms.

That the limiting mass flux
\vec{j}
can be written as
\vec{j}=\rho\vec{v}
is a classical consequence of the functional

\displaystyle E\colon\mathcal{M}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathcal{M}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)\to[0,+\infty],

\displaystyle E(\vec{\nu},\mu)\coloneq\begin{cases}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\!\!\int_{\Omega}\left|\frac{d\vec{\nu}}{d\mu dt}\right|^{2}\,d\mu\,dt&\text{if }d\vec{\nu}\ll d\mu\,dt,\\
+\infty&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

being lower semicontinuous for joint weak-
\ast
convergence of Radon measures
[
AFP00
, Theorem 2.34]
, which follows from the convexity and superlinearity of
\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni p\mapsto\left|p\right|^{2}
. The weak convergences from (i) imply the joint weak-
\ast
convergence, so combining this lower semicontinuity with the fact that the contribution to the dissipation from the continuity equation is bounded

E(\vec{j},\rho)\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\!\!\!\!\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{v}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|^{2}\,d\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\,dt\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\mathscr{D}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\,dt\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}}]<+\infty

we deduce the existence of
\vec{v}\in L^{2}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega,d\rho\,dt;\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\vec{j}\,dx\,dt=d\vec{j}=\vec{v}\,d\rho\,dt=\vec{v}\rho\,dx\,dt
as
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued Radon measures on
\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega
. Thus,
\vec{j}=\vec{v}\rho
a.e. on
\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega
.

∎

4.2.
Phase separation of the density
\rho_{\varepsilon}
and chemoattractant
\phi_{\varepsilon}
(Proof of Theorem
2.2
-
(i)
)

The convergence of the continuity equation did not directly depend on the contribution of the chemoattractant
\phi_{\varepsilon}
to the velocity
\vec{v}_{\varepsilon}
, but only on the uniform-in-
\varepsilon
bound on the flux
\vec{j}_{\varepsilon}
through the dissipation. For phase separation, we will need a further estimate on the chemoattractant.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}})\right\}
be well-prepared
(WP)
and let
(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})
solve (

P
ε

) with initial data
(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}})
. There exist a constant
C({\overline{\mathscr{G}}})>0
that
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))}\leq C({\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}},{\overline{\mathscr{G}}})
.

Proof.

The energy dissipation inequality and well-preparedness give
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\leq{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}
uniformly in
n
and
t
, so Lemma
3.5
-
(ii)
provides

\left\|\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\leq C\big({\overline{\mathscr{G}}}\sup_{n}\varepsilon_{n}\big)^{1/q}+C\qquad\text{for all }n\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }t\in[0,T].

The upper bound is independent of
t
, so the estimate follows.

∎

We show the limiting density
\rho
and chemoattractant
\phi
are phase separated.
Demonstrating this requires to show some strong
L^{1}
-convergence of either the density or chemoattractant. The strategy is similar to that in proof of the compactness result (Proposition
3.6
). The Modica-Mortola functional
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon}
controlled by
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}
naturally bounds in
BV(\Omega)
the auxiliary functions
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
at each fixed time, where
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\coloneq F(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x))
and
F
was defined in (
3.2
) (see also Lemma
3.4
). The energy dissipation inequality satisfied by
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}
(
2.19
) is then responsible for the control over time, which allows to derive strong convergence of
\left\{\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
. The coupling of
(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})
through the penalty
\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{\Omega}[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}g]^{\ast}(\rho_{\varepsilon})-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}+[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}g](\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\,dx
in
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
allows to deduce the
L^{1}
convergence of
\left\{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
from that of
\left\{\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
.

Proposition 4.4

(phase separation)
.

Let
\left\{\varepsilon_{n}\right\}
be a positive sequence converging to zero and
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\}
be weak solutions of (

P
ε

) with well-prepared initial data
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{in}})\right\}

(WP)
and
\mathsf{c}
satisfying
(C1)
. Let
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\coloneq F(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x))
with
F\colon\mathbb{R}\to\left[0,+\infty\right[
from Lemma
3.4
. Then there exists

\phi\in C^{\frac{1}{2}}([0,T];L^{1}(\Omega))\cap C^{\frac{1}{2}-\theta}([0,T];L^{p}(\Omega))\cap BV(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)\cap L^{\infty}(0,T;BV(\Omega))

for all arbitrarily small
\theta>0
and any
1\leq p<+\infty
such that, up to a subsequence,

(4.4)

\displaystyle\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\gamma\phi\text{ strongly in }C([0,T];L^{r}(\Omega))\text{ for all }1\leq r<q;

(4.5)

\displaystyle\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\rho\text{ strongly in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{s}(\Omega))\text{ for all }1\leq s<m\text{ and weakly-}\ast\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{m}(\Omega));

(4.6)

\displaystyle\text{and }\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\phi\text{ strongly in }C([0,T];L^{r}(\Omega))\text{ for all }1\leq r<q\text{ and weakly-}\ast\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega)).

Furthermore, there exists a family
\left\{E(t)\right\}_{t\in[0,T]}
of measurable subsets of
\overline{\Omega}
satisfying

(4.7)

\displaystyle\forall\,t\in[0,T]\qquad\rho(t)=\frac{\rho_{+}}{\phi_{+}}\phi(t)=\rho_{+}\chi_{E(t)}\text{ a.e.\ in }\Omega,\quad\left|E(t)\right|=\frac{1}{\rho_{+}},

(4.8)

\displaystyle\chi_{\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{0}}\chi_{E(t)}=0\text{ a.e.\ in }\Omega,\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Per(E(t),\Omega)<+\infty.

We recall
[
Mag12
, Proposition 12.35]
that sets of finite perimeter with finite volume satisfy the isoperimetric inequality
\Per(E)\geq d\omega_{d}^{1/d}\left|E\right|^{(d-1)/d}
, where
\omega_{d}
is the volume of the Euclidean unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
(
d\geq 2
). Since our sets
\left\{E(t)\right\}_{t}
have a finite, positive, constant volume (
4.7
), their perimeter is also bounded away from zero uniformly in time.

Proof.

We show some precompactness for
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
in
C([0,T];L^{1}(\Omega))
using Arzelà-Ascoli. As part of this aim, we show that
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;BV(\Omega))
and
BV(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
.

At a fixed
t\in[0,T]
, we use that
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\coloneq F\circ\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}
for Lipschitz
F
(Lemma
3.4
), so the uniform-in-
\varepsilon_{n}

L^{\infty}_{t}L^{q}_{x}
-bound on the chemoattractant (Lemma
4.3
) gives

\left\|\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\leq\left\|F^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\left\|\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\leq\left\|F^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))}.

Then
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))
and thus also in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))
.

Keeping time suppressed, we estimate
\left\{\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
in
L^{1}(\Omega)^{d}
. The uniform ellipticity of
{\mathsf{A}}

(A2)
, the chain rule, and Young’s inequality, give

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x)\right|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.33334pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-2.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.66667pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-1.66667pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}=\left|\sqrt{2W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x))}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x))+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}^{2}.

After an integration over
x\in\Omega
, the second term is
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]
, which is bounded uniformly in
t
and
n
by
{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}
due to Lemma
4.1
-
(i)
. Thus,
\left\{\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega)^{d})
, which completes the proof that
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;BV(\Omega))
.

To additionally show
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
is bounded in
BV(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
, it remains to estimate
\left\{\partial_{t}\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
.
Cauchy-Schwarz, the inequality
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\cdot]\leq\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\cdot]
, and the energy dissipation inequality (
2.19
) give an
L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))
-bound on
\left\{\partial_{t}\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|\,dx\right)^{2}dt=\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\Omega}\sqrt{2W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})}\left|\partial_{t}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|\,dx\right)^{2}dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\,dx\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}\varepsilon_{n}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\big)^{2}\,dx\right)\,dt\leq 2\int_{0}^{T}\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\mathscr{D}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\,dt\leq 2{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}^{2}.

In view of the previous
L^{\infty}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
-bounds, we conclude
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
is bounded in
BV(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
.

The above bounds also show
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
is bounded in
H^{1}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))\hookrightarrow C^{\frac{1}{2}}([0,T];L^{1}(\Omega))
. Identifying each term with its Hölder continuous representative, the
L^{\infty}(0,T;BV(\Omega))
-bound implies
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right\}_{(t,n)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
BV(\Omega)
, hence precompact in
L^{1}(\Omega)
. Then Arzelà-Ascoli provides
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
is precompact in
C([0,T];L^{1}(\Omega))
. We thus extract a subsequence that converges to, say,
\gamma\phi\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\Omega))
, where
\gamma
is defined in (
2.14
). The uniform bound on the Hölder constants implies the Hölder continuity passes to the limit, so
\phi\in C^{\frac{1}{2}}([0,T];L^{1}(\Omega))
. The lower semicontinuity of the
BV(\Omega)
-seminorm and
L^{\infty}(0,T;BV(\Omega))
-bound (resp.
BV(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
-seminorm and
BV(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
-bound) implies
\phi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;BV(\Omega))
(resp.
\phi\in BV(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
).

As in the proof of Proposition
3.6
, we can transfer this convergence to
\left\{\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
. By fixing
t\in[0,T]
and applying the same argument, for any small
\delta>0
there exists a constant
C_{\delta}>0
(independent of
n,t
and depending only on
\delta,F,W_{\ast}
) for which

\left\|\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)-\gamma\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\leq C\left|\Omega\right|^{1/q^{\prime}}\delta+C_{\delta}\varepsilon_{n}\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\leq C\delta+C_{\delta}{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}\varepsilon_{n}.

The right-hand side is independent of
t
, so taking the supremum over
t
, sending
n\to\infty
, and then
\delta\to 0^{+}
, we obtain

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left\|\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\gamma\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega))}=0.

Thus, the strong convergence of
\left\{\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
implies
\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\to\phi
in
C([0,T];L^{1}(\Omega))
. This implies for each
t\in[0,T]
that
\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\to\phi(t)
strongly in
L^{1}(\Omega)
and (up to another subsequence) a.e. in
\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega
.

Using this strong convergence, we can establish phase separation of the chemoattractant. By continuity of
W_{\ast}
, we note for each
t\in[0,T]
that
W_{\ast}\circ\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\to W_{\ast}\circ\phi(t)
a.e. in
\Omega
. Then nonnegativity of
W_{\ast}
, Fatou’s lemma, and that
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\leq\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\leq{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}
uniformly in
n
and
t
give

\int_{\Omega}W_{\ast}(\phi(t))\,dx\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t))\,dx\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\big(\varepsilon_{n}\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\big)\leq{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}\liminf_{n\to\infty}\varepsilon_{n}=0.

Thus,
W_{\ast}(\phi(t))=0
a.e. in
\Omega
, and since
W_{\ast}
vanishes only on
\left\{0,\phi_{+}\right\}
, there exists a measurable set
E(t)\subset\Omega
such that
\phi(t)=\phi_{+}\chi_{E(t)}
a.e. in
\Omega
. Since
\phi(t)\in BV(\Omega)
, this means
E(t)
has finite perimeter in
\Omega
. Since
\phi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;BV(\Omega))
, this means the perimeter is uniformly bounded:
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Per(E(t),\Omega)<+\infty
.

Now that we know for each
t\in[0,T]
that
\phi=\phi_{+}\chi_{E(t)}
, we have
\phi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))
, so by interpolating
L^{p}(\Omega)
-norms between
1
and arbitrarily large
p_{2}
, we have for each
t_{2},t_{1}\in[0,T]
and
p=(1-\theta)\cdot 1+\theta p_{2}
with
\theta\in\left]0,1\right[
