2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\big)\Big),

so
\mathscr{F}_{n}(t)=\int_{\Omega}v_{n}(t)+u_{n}(t)\,dx
and
\mathscr{G}_{n}(t)=\int_{\Omega}w_{n}(t)+o_{n}(t)+u_{n}(t)\,dx
. Then the Modica-Mortola trick and some algebra give

0\leq\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}=2\sqrt{u_{n}v_{n}}=u_{n}+v_{n}-(\sqrt{u_{n}}-\sqrt{v_{n}})^{2}.

Integrating over
x\in\Omega
and rearranging then provides

\left\|\sqrt{u_{n}}-\sqrt{v_{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}=\mathscr{F}_{n}-\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\,dx\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(0,T),

where the convergence is a consequence of the third and fourth equalities in
(i)
.

By introducing a difference of squares, using Cauchy-Schwarz, recalling the uniform-in-
t
-and-
n
bound on
\mathscr{F}_{n}
(
5.1
), and applying the above limit,

(5.5)

\begin{gathered}\left\|u_{n}-v_{n}\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\left\|\sqrt{u_{n}}+\sqrt{v_{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\left\|\sqrt{u_{n}}-\sqrt{v_{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C(\mathscr{F}_{n})^{1/2}\left\|\sqrt{u_{n}}-\sqrt{v_{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0,\end{gathered}

where the convergence is in
L^{2}(0,T)
-strong.

Now we observe, strongly in
L^{1}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(v_{n}-\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{A}\big)=\frac{1}{2}\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(v_{n}+u_{n}-\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(v_{n}-u_{n}\big)=0.

On the right-hand side, the first limit is a consequence of the third and fourth equalities in
(i)
and the second limit is a consequence of (
5.5
). This establishes the second limit in
(iv)
. By interchanging the roles of
u_{n}
and
v_{n}
in the above argument, we obtain the third limit in
(iv)
.

(v)
: We will prove the first equality for test functions
\zeta\in C(\overline{\Omega})
satisfying
0\leq\zeta(x)\leq 1
for all
x\in\Omega
. The extension to arbitrary
\zeta\in C(\overline{\Omega})
is obtained by linearity.

We find for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
,

\int_{\Omega}\zeta\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t))+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}\Big)\,dx\geq\int_{\Omega}\zeta\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,dx,

and since
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\to\gamma\phi(t)
strongly in
L^{1}(\Omega)
(Proposition
4.4
), the Reshetnyak lower semicontinuity theorem gives, a.e.
t\in[0,T]
,

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}\zeta\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t))+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}^{2}\Big)\,dx\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}\zeta\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,dx\geq\gamma\int_{\Omega}\zeta\left|\vec{\nu}(t)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}d|\nabla\phi(t)|(x).

To obtain the
\limsup
inequalities, we replace
\zeta
by
1-\zeta
in the above inequality and apply the pointwise a.e. convergences
\mathscr{F}_{n}(t),\,\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\,dx\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\mathscr{G}_{0}(t)
from
(i)
.

The second and third limits in
(v)
are obtained by combining the first limit in
(v)
with
the second and third limits in
(iv)
, resp.
∎

Fixing a test function
\zeta\in C(\overline{\Omega})
, part
(v)
gives convergence for pointwise a.e.
t\in[0,T]
. The uniform-in-
t
-and-
\varepsilon
bound on the energy
\mathscr{F}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}]
(
5.1
) and Lebesgue dominated convergence give the following time-dependent version of the weak-
\ast
convergence in
(v)
. We will use it to deduce the convergence of the various terms in the weak formulation of (

P
ε

) to those in our weak formulation of (

P
0

).

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\}
be as in the previous lemma. For any test function
\zeta\in C([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega})

(5.6)

\begin{gathered}\frac{\gamma}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\zeta(t,x)\left|\vec{\nu}(t,x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d|\nabla\phi(t)|(x)\,dt=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\zeta(t,x)\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,dx\,dt\\
=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\zeta(t,x)\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x))\,dx\,dt=\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\zeta(t,x)\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}^{2}\,dx\,dt.\end{gathered}

5.2.
The normal component of the velocity of the free boundary (Proof of identity (
2.22
) in Theorem
2.2
-
(iii)
)

The aim of this section is to demonstrate that the limiting velocity
\vec{v}
derived in Proposition
4.2
-
(ii)
admits, in a weak sense, a normal trace on the free boundary. This is handled through methods that are classical for the derivation of
BV
solutions to mean-curvature flow as a singular limit of strong solutions of Allen-Cahn-type equations (see, e.g.,
[
LS18
, Proposition 2.10]
,
[
HL24
, Section 3.1.3]
,
[
LSU24
, Lemma 4.4]
, or
[
MR24
, Lemma 7.2]
).

The phase separation result (Proposition
4.4
) shows there is a limit point
(\rho,\phi)
of
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\}
that can be written, for each
t\in[0,T]
, as
\frac{\rho_{+}}{\phi_{+}}\phi(t)=\rho(t)=\rho_{+}\chi_{E(t)}
, where
E(t)\subset\overline{\Omega}
is a set of finite perimeter in
\Omega
. The following result shows
\phi
satisfies a distributional formulation of a level set equation with normal velocity
V
.

Lemma 5.4

(existence of the normal velocity
V
)
.

There exists a constant
C({\overline{\mathscr{G}}})>0
such that, if the sequence
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\}
from the phase separation result and its limit
(\rho,\phi)
satisfy the energy convergence assumption
(EC)
, then for all test functions
\zeta\in C^{1}_{c}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
we have

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\phi(t,x)\partial_{t}\zeta(t,x)\,dx\,dt\right|\leq C\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\zeta(t,x)\,d|\nabla\phi(t)|(x)\,dt\right)^{1/2}.

In particular, there exists
V\in L^{2}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega,\left|\nabla\phi\right|dt)
such that
\partial_{t}\phi=V\left|\nabla\phi\right|\,dt
as Radon measures on
[0,T]\times\overline{\Omega}
and thus as distributions in the following sense:

(5.7)

\forall\,\zeta\in C^{1}_{c}(\left[0,T\right[\times\overline{\Omega})\qquad\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\phi\partial_{t}\zeta\,dx\,dt+\int_{\Omega}\phi(0,x)\zeta(0,x)\,dx=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}V\zeta\,d|\nabla\phi(t)|(x)\,dt.

Moreover, for any
\zeta\in C^{1}_{c}(\left]0,T\right[\times\overline{\Omega})
, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\zeta\,dx\,dt=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}\zeta V\,d|\nabla\phi|\,dt
.

Proof.

We refer to
[
MR24
, Lemma 7.2]
for a proof.
∎

To derive (
2.22
), the second equation in our weak formulation of (

P
0

), we recall
\rho=\frac{\rho_{+}}{\phi_{+}}\phi
. Because
(\rho,\vec{v})
satisfies (
2.21
) and
(\phi,V)
satisfies (
5.7
), a linear combination of these equations gives (
2.22
). Thus,
V(t)
is equal (in a weak sense) to the normal component of
\vec{v}(t)
on the free boundary
\partial E(t)
.

6.
Derivation of Hele-Shaw flow with surface tension and kinetic undercooling

In this section, we show that a potential for the organism velocity
\vec{v}_{\varepsilon}=-\nabla\big(f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon})+\mathsf{a}-\phi_{\varepsilon}\big)/\varepsilon
leads to the free boundary condition
p(t)+\gamma_{0}\nabla p(t)\cdot\vec{\nu}(t)=\gamma_{0}\kappa_{\mathsf{A}}(t)
on
\partial E(t)
, where
\kappa_{\mathsf{A}}
is the anisotropic mean-curvature of the free boundary. In view of the velocity law
V(t)=-\nabla p(t)\cdot\vec{\nu}(t)
on
\partial E(t)
, it is equivalent to show
p(t)=\gamma_{0}V(t)+\gamma_{0}\kappa_{\mathsf{A}}(t)
on
\partial E(t)
. We first show how the undercooling and curvature terms arise and then we apply this to derive (
2.24
), the final part of our weak formulation of (

P
0

).

6.1.
Derivation of the undercooling and curvature terms

Theorem
2.1
shows the energy
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}
(
2.8
) dissipated by the system
\Gamma
-converges for
L^{1}(\Omega)^{2}
-strong to a weighted perimeter
\mathscr{G}_{0}
(
2.13
). The phase separation result (Proposition
4.4
) provides, essentially uniformly in time, the strong
L^{1}(\Omega)^{2}
-convergence of a subsequence of solutions to the system:
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}(\rho_{\varepsilon},\phi_{\varepsilon})=(\rho,\phi)
, where
\rho(t)=(\rho_{+}/\phi_{+})\phi(t)=\rho_{+}\chi_{E(t)}
. The energy convergence assumption
(EC)
provides that the time-integrated energies (evaluated along this converging subsequence) also converge:
\int_{0}^{T}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}[\rho_{\varepsilon}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon}(t)]\,dt\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathscr{G}_{0}[\rho(t),\phi(t)]\,dt
. We would then like to show, as a consequence of these limits, first variations of the energies
\left\{\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}\right\}
evaluated along
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon},\phi_{\varepsilon})\right\}
converges to a first variation of the limit energy
\mathscr{G}_{0}
evaluated at the limit
(\rho,\phi)
.

Proposition 6.1

(convergence of first variations)
.

Suppose
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\}
satisfies the conclusion of the phase separation result (Proposition
4.4
) with limit
(\rho,\phi)
and energy convergence
(EC)
is satisfied. Let
\mathsf{c}
satisfy
(C1)
, and let
\vec{n}
and
\vec{n}_{0}
denote the outer unit normals to
\partial\Omega
and
(\partial\Omega_{0})\cap\Omega
, resp. Then for every test vector field
\xi\in C^{1}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfying
\xi\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
\partial\Omega
and
\xi\cdot\vec{n}_{0}=0
on
(\partial\Omega_{0})\cap\Omega
:

(6.1)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big[f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})+\mathsf{a}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle=\gamma_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}V\vec{\nu}\cdot\xi\,d|\nabla\rho(t)|(x)\,dt

\displaystyle+\gamma_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\Big[\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\frac{A\vec{\nu}}{\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{\vec{\nu}}{\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\colon(\nabla\xi)^{\top}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\vec{\nu}}{\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{\vec{\nu}}{\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\colon\big(\xi^{k}\partial_{k}A\big)\Big]\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\,d|\nabla\rho(t)|(x)\,dt,

where
\gamma_{0}\coloneq\gamma\phi_{+}/\rho_{+}
and
-\vec{\nu}(t)
is the Radon-Nikodym derivative of
\nabla\rho(t)
w.r.t.
\left|\nabla\rho(t)\right|
.

The quantity on the first line of (
6.1
) being integrated over time is, formally,
\frac{d}{ds}\big|_{s=0}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}[\rho_{s},\phi_{\varepsilon}]
, where
\rho_{s}
is a perturbation of
\rho_{\varepsilon}
by the flow of the transport equation:
\partial_{s}\rho_{s}+\xi\cdot\nabla\rho_{s}=0
with
\rho_{0}=\rho_{\varepsilon}
for a prescribed velocity field
\xi
. This perturbation is motivated by the fact that the distributional anisotropic mean-curvature arises is the first variation of the weighted perimeter
\mathscr{G}_{0}
w.r.t. these perturbations (domain variations). We perturb the first variable of
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}
as opposed to its second variable (or both variables simultaneously) since, for our scaling of (

P
ε

), the dynamics corresponding to the first equation of (

P
ε

) are slower than those of the second equation, so we expect them to dominate for
\varepsilon\ll 1
.

We require that the test vector fields
\xi
satisfy
\xi\cdot\vec{n}_{0}=0
on
(\partial\Omega_{0})\cap\Omega
. We impose this restriction since all functions in the domain of the limit
\mathscr{G}_{0}
are supported on
\Omega_{0}
, so if
\rho
is supported on
\Omega_{0}
, then perturbations of
\rho
by the flow of the transport equation with velocity field
\xi
will also be supported on
\Omega_{0}
. We note that the domain of
\mathscr{G}_{0}
is not invariant under such a large class of perturbations. For instance, these do not necessarily preserve the mass,
1=\int_{\Omega}\rho\,dx\neq\int_{\Omega}\rho_{s}\,dx
, since they do not not necessarily preserve the volume,
\rho_{+}^{-1}=\left|E\right|\neq\left|E_{s}\right|
, unless we further restrict to divergence-free vector fields.

The integral on the second line of (
6.1
) is a weak formulation of the so-called undercooling term in (

P
0

), i.e., the second term in the fourth equation of (

P
0

), while the third line of (
6.1
) is a weak formulation of the anisotropic mean-curvature
\kappa_{\mathsf{A}}
that appears in the fourth equation of (

P
0

). In the limit, we obtain more than just the first variation of the limit energy (namely, the undercooling term) because we evaluate the energy along solutions
(\rho_{\varepsilon},\phi_{\varepsilon})
to (

P
ε

), as opposed to, say, minimizers of
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}
.

The proof of Proposition
6.1
is facilitated by rewriting the first line of (
6.1
) in a manner more amenable to passing to the limit. The aim is to introduce terms whose convergence is addressed by the equipartition of the energy or Reshetnyak continuity.

Lemma 6.2

(first variation)
.

For
\left\{(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\}
as in the previous proposition (but not necessarily satisfying the energy convergence assumption), we have for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
, which we suppress, and all
\xi\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{d})
for which
\xi\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
\partial\Omega

(6.2)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}\big[f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})

\displaystyle+\mathsf{a}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon_{n}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\big[W(\rho)+[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho)-\rho\phi+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi)\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|^{2}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{n}\int_{\Omega}(({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\otimes\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}):(\nabla\xi)^{\top}\,dx+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\otimes\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\colon(\xi^{k}\partial_{k}A)\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\nabla\mathsf{c}\cdot\xi\,dx.

Assume
(C1)
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
\overline{\mathscr{G}}\coloneq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho^{\mathrm{in}}_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi^{\mathrm{in}}_{\varepsilon_{n}}]
,
T
, and independent of
n
such that for all test vector fields
\xi\in C^{1}([0,T]\times\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfying
\xi\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
\partial\Omega
and
\xi\cdot\vec{n}_{0}=0
on
(\partial\Omega_{0})\cap\Omega

(6.3)

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big[f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})+\mathsf{a}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt\right|\leq C\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}))}.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

Since we work with a fixed
n
when proving the identity (
6.2
), we omit
\varepsilon_{n}
.

We use the definition of
W
(
2.2
), add and subtract each of
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi)
,
\mathsf{c}g(\phi)
to the quantity in brackets in the first line of (
6.2
), and integrate by parts to obtain

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega}\big[W(\rho)+[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi)\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(\phi)\mathrm{div}\,\xi+g(\phi)\nabla\mathsf{c}\cdot\xi\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega}(\mathsf{c}g^{\prime}(\phi)-\rho)\nabla\phi\cdot\xi\,dx.

Since
\phi
is a strong solution of
\varepsilon\partial_{t}\phi-\varepsilon\mathrm{div}\left({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi\right)=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}(\mathsf{c}g^{\prime}(\phi)-\rho)
, the integral on the second line is

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega}\big[W(\rho)+[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho)-\rho\phi+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi)\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(\phi)\mathrm{div}\,\xi+g(\phi)\nabla\mathsf{c}\cdot\xi\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad-\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\phi\nabla\phi\cdot\xi-\mathrm{div}\left({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi\right)\nabla\phi\cdot\xi\,dx.

We manipulate the divergence term on the second line and show it gives rise to terms appearing in the distributional formulation of the anisotropic mean-curvature. In the following, we use that on
\partial\Omega
both
\xi\cdot\vec{n}=0
and
{\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi\cdot\vec{n}=0
(no-flux boundary condition).

Throughout, we employ the Einstein summation convention. By several integrations by parts,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{div}\left({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi\right)\nabla\phi\cdot\xi\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\partial_{i}\big({\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{j}\phi\big)\partial_{k}\phi\xi^{k}\,dx=-\int_{\Omega}{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{i}(\partial_{k}\phi\xi^{k})\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{k}\partial_{i}\phi\xi^{k}+{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{k}\phi\partial_{i}\xi^{k}\,dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{j}\phi\big[\partial_{k}(\partial_{i}\phi\xi^{k})-\partial_{i}\phi\partial_{k}\xi^{k}\big]\,dx-\int_{\Omega}\Tr\big[(({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi)\otimes\nabla\phi)\nabla\xi\big]\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\phi\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\Tr\big[(({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi)\otimes\nabla\phi)\nabla\xi\big]\,dx+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{i}\phi\partial_{k}{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{j}\phi\xi^{k}+\partial_{i}\phi{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{k}\partial_{j}\phi\xi^{k}\,dx.

We note that the symmetry of
{\mathsf{A}}
implies that the term
\partial_{i}\phi{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{k}\partial_{j}\phi\xi^{k}
is invariant under the interchange of indices
i\leftrightarrow j
, so
\partial_{i}\phi{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{k}\partial_{j}\phi\xi^{k}=\frac{1}{2}{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\big(\partial_{i}\phi\partial_{k}\partial_{j}\phi+\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{k}\partial_{i}\phi\big)\xi^{k}=\frac{1}{2}{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{k}(\partial_{i}\phi\partial_{j}\phi)\xi^{k}
. Substituting this and integrating by parts once more provides

\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{div}\left({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi\right)\nabla\phi\cdot\xi\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla\phi\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\Tr\big[(({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi)\otimes\nabla\phi)\nabla\xi\big]+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{i}\phi\partial_{k}{\mathsf{A}}^{i,j}\partial_{j}\phi\xi^{k}\,dx,

which establishes the identity (
6.2
).

For the bound (
6.3
), we control the right-hand side of (
6.2
) line-by-line. To control the second line, Cauchy-Schwarz, the energy dissipation inequality, and the upper bound on the initial energies give

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon_{n}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt\right|\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\varepsilon_{n}\partial_{t}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|\right\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\left[\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\Omega}\varepsilon_{n}\big(\partial_{t}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\big)^{2}\,dx\right)\left(\varepsilon_{n}\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|^{2}\,dx\right)\,dt\right]^{1/2}\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\int_{0}^{T}\mathscr{D}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)\,dt\right]^{1/2}\big[2\overline{{\mathsf{A}}}\,\overline{\mathscr{G}}\big]^{1/2}\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\leq[2\overline{{\mathsf{A}}}]^{1/2}\,\overline{\mathscr{G}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}.

For the third line in (
6.2
), we recognize the quantities as contributions to
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\big(W(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})+[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})+\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}\big)\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx\,dt\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\left\|\nabla\xi(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,dt\leq\sqrt{T}\,\overline{\mathscr{G}}\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}.

For the first integral in the fourth line of (
6.2
), we fix
t\in[0,T]
, multiply and divide by
\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}
, and use Cauchy-Schwarz for the Frobenius inner product
\left|A:B\right|\leq\left|A\right|_{F}\left|B\right|_{F}
to obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{n}\int_{\Omega}\left|({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\otimes(\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}):(\nabla\xi)^{\top}\right|\,dx\leq\int_{\Omega}\left|\frac{({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\right|_{F}\left|\nabla\xi\right|_{F}\big(\varepsilon_{n}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}\big)\,dx.

Note, for
p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have (using
(A2)
,
(A1)
)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{{\mathsf{A}}p}{\left|p\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{p}{\left|p\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\right|_{F}=\frac{1}{\left|p\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}}\Big[\sum_{i=1}^{d}(({\mathsf{A}}p)^{i})^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(p^{j})^{2}\Big]^{1/2}=\frac{\left|{\mathsf{A}}p\right|}{\left|p\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\frac{\left|p\right|}{\left|p\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\leq\frac{\overline{{\mathsf{A}}}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 3.33334pt\hskip-3.33334pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-3.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 3.33334pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.33334pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-2.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.66667pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-1.66667pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}},

so we can bound the previous integral by
C\frac{\overline{{\mathsf{A}}}}{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.33334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-2.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.33334pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-2.33334pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.63333pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-1.63333pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.16667pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathsf{A}}$}\hskip-1.16667pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{{\mathsf{A}}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\left\|\nabla\xi(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]
. Then, after an integration over
t\in[0,T]
, we obtain the estimate

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon_{n}\int_{\Omega}({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\otimes(\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}):(\nabla\xi)^{\top}\,dx\,dt\right|\leq C\sqrt{T}\,\overline{\mathscr{G}}\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}.

Estimating the second integral on the fourth line of (
6.2
) is similar and uses that
A\in C^{1}(\overline{\Omega})

(A1)
:

\left|\varepsilon_{n}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\otimes\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}:(\xi^{k}\partial_{k}A)\,dx\,dt\right|\leq C\sqrt{T}\,\overline{\mathscr{G}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}.

On the fifth line of (
6.2
), the first integral has a contribution to
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
, so we control this integral by

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}{\varepsilon_{n}}g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx\,dt\right|\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\left\|\nabla\xi(t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\,dt\leq\sqrt{T}\,\overline{\mathscr{G}}\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}.

Finally, we control the second integral on the fifth line of (
6.2
). For this purpose, we use
(C1)
to bound it with
\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\,dx
and it is at this stage that we use
\xi\cdot\vec{n}_{0}=0
on
(\partial\Omega_{0})\cap\Omega
. Recall
\chi_{\Omega_{0}}\nabla\mathsf{c}=0
a.e., so what remains is an estimate over the region
\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{0}
. Let
U
be the neighborhood of
(\partial\Omega_{0})\cap\Omega
from
(C1)
, and then at a.e.
t\in[0,T]

(6.4)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\left|\nabla\mathsf{c}\cdot\xi\right|\,dx\leq\frac{K_{4}\left\|\xi\right\|_{C^{1}(\overline{U})}}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{U}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\,dx\qquad+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{(\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0})\setminus U}g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})\left|\nabla\ln(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})\right|\left|\xi\right|\,dx.

The first term on the right-hand side is now controlled by
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
. For the second term on the right-hand side, we note
\ln(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})
is Lipschitz on
(\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0})\setminus U
since
\mathsf{c}
is bounded away from

\textstyle\mathsf{c}
\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}

on this region and
\mathsf{c}
is Lipschitz, so
\left|\nabla\ln(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})\right|\in L^{\infty}((\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0})\setminus U)
. Thus (
6.4
) is bounded by

C\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]\left\|\xi(t)\right\|_{C^{1}(\overline{\Omega})},

and then integrating over
t\in[0,T]
provides

\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\nabla\mathsf{c}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt\right|\leq C\sqrt{T}\,\overline{\mathscr{G}}\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}))}.

This completes the proof of (
6.3
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

Using the previous lemma, we instead pass to the limit in the right-hand side of (
6.2
). Throughout, we use that the limit
(\rho,\phi)
satisfies
\phi=\frac{\phi_{+}}{\rho_{+}}\rho
.

For the second line in (
6.2
), one uses the same reasoning as for the elliptic-parabolic system
[
MR24
, Proposition 8.1]
or the convergence of strong solutions of Allen-Cahn-type equations to
BV
solutions of mean-curvature flow. We omit the proof, which is the analogous to the one given in
[
MR24
, Proposition 8.1]
. This gives

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\varepsilon_{n}\partial_{t}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt=\gamma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}V\xi\cdot d\nabla\phi(t)\,dt=-\gamma_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}V\vec{\nu}\cdot\xi\,d|\nabla\rho(t)|\,dt.

The quantities in the third line of (
6.2
) are the contributions to
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
, and their convergence is handled by the equipartition of the energy. In particular, Lemma
5.1
-
(iii)
and Lebesgue dominated convergence permits us to replace
W(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})+[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})
by
W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})
up to some asymptotically negligible error, and then the second equality of (
5.6
) in Corollary
5.3
provides

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big[W(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})+[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx\,dt=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}W_{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,d|\nabla\phi(t)|\,dt=\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{div}\,\xi\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\,d|\nabla\rho(t)|\,dt.

The convergence of the Dirichlet-like energy is handled directly by the equipartition of the energy, the third equality in (
5.6
) of Corollary
5.3
.

The convergence of each of the integrals in the fourth line of (
6.2
) is handled by a version of the Reshetnyak continuity theorem adapted to the anisotropic setting
[
CNP10
, Lemma 3.7]
. We recall that the classical Reshetnyak continuity theorem
[
AFP00
, Theorem 2.39]
requires the convergence of the total variations
\int_{\Omega}d|\nabla u_{n}|\to\int_{\Omega}d|\nabla u|
, which we do not have in our setting. Instead, we have the convergence of some anisotropic total variations. Indeed, the last equality of Lemma
5.1
-
(i)
provides

\text{for a.e.\ }t\in[0,T]\qquad\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,dx=\gamma\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{\nu}(t,x)\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}\,d\left|\nabla\phi(t)\right|(x).

We show only the convergence of the first term on the fourth line of (
6.2
) since the other term is analogous. For a fixed but suppressed time
t\in[0,T]
,

\varepsilon_{n}\int_{\Omega}\Tr\big[(({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\otimes\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})(\nabla\xi)\big]\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\frac{{\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\colon(\nabla\xi)^{\top}(\varepsilon_{n}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2})\,dx.

Since each of the components of
{\mathsf{A}}
and
\nabla\xi
are continuous up to the boundary, and because the
{\mathsf{A}}(x)
-norm is, uniformly in
x
, comparable to the standard
2
-norm
(A1)
,
(A2)
, we have that

\frac{{\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\colon(\nabla\xi)^{\top}\text{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)\text{ uniformly in }n.

By equipartition of the energy (Lemma
5.1
-
(iv)
), we have
\varepsilon_{n}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}-\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\to 0
in
L^{1}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
, so combining the above bound with this limit shows, up to some asymptotically negligible error,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{{\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\colon(\nabla\xi)^{\top}(\varepsilon_{n}\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}^{2})\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\frac{{\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\colon(\nabla\xi)^{\top}\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\,dx+o_{n\to\infty}(1).

We now reintroduce the auxiliary function
\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\coloneq F(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})
, where
F^{\prime}(v)=\sqrt{2W_{\ast}(v)}\geq 0
. Using that
F^{\prime}\geq 0
, we find

=\int_{\Omega}\frac{{\mathsf{A}}\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}}{\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\colon(\nabla\xi)^{\top}\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\,dx+o_{n\to\infty}(1).

Since phase separation (Proposition
4.4
) and equipartition of the energy (Lemma
5.1
-
(i)
) give

\displaystyle\text{for each }t\in[0,T]\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}=\gamma\phi\text{ in }L^{1}(\Omega)\,\text{ and }\,\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}\left|\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\,dx=\gamma\int_{\Omega}\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\,d|\nabla\phi|,

the Reshetnyak continuity-type theorem
[
CNP10
, Lemma 3.7]
provides, for each
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\varepsilon_{n}\int_{\Omega}\Tr\big[(({\mathsf{A}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\otimes\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\nabla\xi\big]\,dx=\gamma\int_{\Omega}\frac{A\vec{\nu}}{\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\otimes\frac{\vec{\nu}}{\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}}\colon(\nabla\xi)^{\top}\left|\vec{\nu}\right|_{\mathsf{A}}\,d|\nabla\phi|.

Lebesgue dominated convergence yields the convergence after integration over
t\in[0,T]
.

It remains to show that the fifth line of (
6.2
) vanishes as
n\to\infty
. In particular, strongly in
L^{1}(0,T)
,

\begin{gathered}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\nabla\mathsf{c}\cdot\xi\,dx=0.\end{gathered}

The first limit is an immediate consequence of equipartition of the energy, Lemma
5.1
-
(ii)
. For the second limit, the nondegeneracy condition
(C1)
and
\xi\cdot\vec{n}_{0}=0
on
\partial\Omega_{0}
imply
\left|\nabla\mathsf{c}\cdot\xi\right|\leq K_{4}\left\|\xi\right\|_{C^{1}(\overline{U})}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})
on some neighborhood
U
of
\partial\Omega_{0}
as in (
6.4
), and then the convergence is handled by Lemma
5.1
-
(ii)
.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

The Reshetnyak continuity-type theorem
[
CNP10
, Lemma 3.7]
is proven for a sequence of
C^{1}(\Omega)
functions
\left\{v_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
that converges strongly in
L^{1}(\Omega)
to some
v_{0}\in BV(\Omega)
and for which some inhomogeneous, anisotropic total variations converge
\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}\phi(x,\nabla v_{\varepsilon_{n}})\,dx=\int_{\Omega}\phi(x,\vec{\nu}_{0})\,d|\nabla v_{0}|
,
where
\vec{\nu}_{0}
is the Radon-Nikodym derivative of
\nabla v_{0}
w.r.t. its total variation
\left|\nabla v_{0}\right|
and
\phi
is a smooth, strictly convex Finsler norm, of which
(x,p)\mapsto\left|p\right|_{{\mathsf{A}}(x)}
is an example. For more on Finsler norms and their use in the variational setting of anisotropic mean-curvature flow, see,
[
BP96
]
,
[
CNP10
, Section 2]
, or
[
LSU24
]
. They derive
\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}F(x,\nabla v_{\varepsilon_{n}})\,dx=\int_{\Omega}F(x,\vec{\nu}_{0})\,d|\nabla v_{0}|

for
F\in C(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
that is positively
1
-homogeneous in the second variable and vanishes off of some compact set in the first variable. The vanishing condition is not necessary for their proof, and the result can be extended by density to sequences
\left\{v_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}\subset W^{1,1}(\Omega)
provided
F
is also Lipschitz in its second variable uniformly in its first variable, which is the case we use the result.

6.2.
Derivation of the limiting pressure equation (Derivation of (
2.24
) in Theorem
2.2
-
(iii)
)

Recall the weak formulation of the mass flux
\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\coloneq\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\vec{v}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
when viewing the evolution equation for the density as a continuity equation:

(6.5)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt=\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}\alpha_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi+\big[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx\,dt.

The estimate on the mass flux, Lemma
4.1
-
(ii)
, provides that the left-hand side is bounded over
n
:

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt\right|\leq\big(\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))}\big)\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d}))},

and Proposition
4.2
-
(i)
shows
\left\{\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
converges weakly to some
\vec{j}\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{2m}{m+1}}(\Omega);\mathbb{R}^{d})
that can be written as
\vec{j}=\rho\vec{v}
for some velocity field
\vec{v}\in L^{2}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega,d\rho dt;\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Consequently, the right-hand side also has a limit as
n\to\infty
, although the individual terms are not immediately bounded.

To circumvent this, we introduce an approximate pressure

\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\coloneq\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\frac{f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x))+\mathsf{a}-\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)}{\varepsilon_{n}},

so the above weak formulation of the flux (
6.5
) becomes

(6.6)

\begin{gathered}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}\alpha_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big[f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})+\mathsf{a}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt\\
+\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx\,dt,\end{gathered}

where we use
\xi\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. Our final aim is to pass to the limit
n\to\infty
in (
6.6
) and thus derive (
2.24
), the final equation in our weak formulation of (

P
0

). Lemma
6.2
shows the first term on the right-hand side can be estimated in terms of
\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{1}(\overline{\Omega};\mathbb{R}^{d}))}
, and Proposition
6.1
shows it converges to the undercooling and curvature terms. Thus, the last term converges, but to identify the limit we need to bound
\left\{\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
. In the following, we use duality to obtain such a bound and pass to the limit, first in the case of
\mathsf{c}
a positive constant function and second in the case of
\mathsf{c}
satisfying
(C1)
,
(C1)
but for the particular case of porous medium diffusion, i.e.,
f(u)=u^{m}/(m-1)+\iota_{\left[0,+\infty\right[}(u)
with
m>q^{\prime}
.

6.2.1.
Spatially homogeneous destruction

In this section, we suppose
\mathsf{c}\equiv{\underline{\mathsf{c}}}>0
, so there is no confinement effect in the limit since
\Omega_{0}\coloneq\left\{\mathsf{c}={\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\right\}=\overline{\Omega}
. As in proofs of
[
JKM21
, Theorem 3.2]
or
[
KMW23
, Section 5.1]
, given
\varphi\in L^{2}(0,T;C^{0,s}(\overline{\Omega}))
, with
0<s<1
, we solve for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
the potential problem

\begin{cases}\Delta u(t)=\varphi(t)-(\varphi(t))_{\Omega}&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\nabla u(t)\cdot\vec{n}=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

where
(\varphi(t))_{\Omega}\coloneq\frac{1}{\left|\Omega\right|}\int_{\Omega}\varphi(t,x)\,dx
is the mean of
\varphi(t)
over
\Omega
. We take the mean-zero solution, so by Schauder estimates
[
Nar15
]
, we have
\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{2,s}(\overline{\Omega}))}\lesssim\left\|\varphi-(\varphi)_{\Omega}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{0,s}(\overline{\Omega}))}
. We set
\xi\coloneq\nabla u\in L^{2}(0,T;C^{1,s}(\overline{\Omega}))
, which satisfies
\mathrm{div}\,\xi=\Delta u=\varphi-(\varphi)_{\Omega}
on
\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega
and
\xi\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
\left]0,T\right[\times\partial\Omega
.

We test (
6.6
) with this particular
\xi
. Let
p_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\coloneq\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)-(\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t))_{\Omega}
, which satisfies
(p_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t))_{\Omega}=0
for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
. Since
\varphi(t)-(\varphi(t))_{\Omega}
has mean-zero over
\Omega
, the weak formulation of the approximate pressure equation (
6.6
) is equivalently

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}p_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\varphi-(\varphi)_{\Omega})\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}\alpha_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big[f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})+\mathsf{a}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt.

Thus, combining the estimate on the mass flux (Lemma
4.1
-
(ii)
), the estimate on the first variation ((
6.3
) from Lemma
6.2
), and the Schauder estimate shows

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}p_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\varphi-(\varphi)_{\Omega})\,dx\,dt\right|\lesssim\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}))}\coloneq\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{1}(\overline{\Omega}))}\lesssim\left\|\varphi-(\varphi)_{\Omega}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{0,s}(\overline{\Omega}))},

so
\left\{p_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X}^{\ast})
, where
\mathcal{X}\coloneq\left\{\zeta\in C^{0,s}(\overline{\Omega})\,:\,(\zeta)_{\Omega}=0\right\}

is a Banach subspace of
C^{0,s}(\overline{\Omega})
. Consequently,
\left\{p_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
admits a subsequence converging weakly-
\ast
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X}^{\ast})
. Then, after passing to a subsequence, we may pass to the limit in (
6.6
) with
p_{\varepsilon_{n}}
in place of
\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}
for any
\xi\in L^{2}(0,T;C^{1,s}(\overline{\Omega}))
that is, for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
, tangential to
\partial\Omega
.

6.2.2.
Nonhomogeneous destruction

Throughout this section, we suppose
\mathsf{c}
satisfies
(C1)
,
(C1)
and
f(u)=u^{m}/(m-1)+\iota_{\left[0,+\infty\right[}(u)
. We proceed in several steps. We remark that the only step that makes use of this particular choice of
f
is Step 3.

Step 1:
(Rewriting the pressure) For clarity, we introduce the functions
P,R
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}

(6.7)

P(u,v)\coloneq f(u)-u(v-\mathsf{a})+f^{\ast}(v-\mathsf{a})\quad\text{and}\quad R(u,v)\coloneq[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(u)-uv+{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g(v),

which are each nonnegative by the Fenchel-Young inequality. Both of
\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{\Omega}P(\rho_{\varepsilon},\phi_{\varepsilon})\,dx
and
\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{\Omega}R(\rho_{\varepsilon},\phi_{\varepsilon})\,dx
are contributions to the energy
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}[\rho_{\varepsilon},\phi_{\varepsilon}]
(cf. (
2.8
) and (
2.7
), resp.), so in particular the energy dissipation inequality (
2.19
) and well-preparedness of the initial data
(WP)
provide

(6.8)

\left\{\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}P(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\}\text{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega)).

Equipartition of the energy (Lemma
5.1
-
(iii)
) provides

(6.9)

\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}P(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0\text{ strongly in }L^{1}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega).

The approximate pressure
\pi_{\varepsilon}
is related to
\partial_{u}P
, the partial subdifferential of
P
w.r.t. its first variable while the second variable is fixed. To isolate the multi-valued part of
\partial f
, we define
\widetilde{f}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\left[0,+\infty\right[
to be
\widetilde{f}(u)\coloneq f(u)
if
u\geq 0
and
\widetilde{f}(u)\coloneq f(-u)
if
u<0
, so
f=\widetilde{f}+\iota_{\left[0,+\infty\right[}
. By
(F1)
,
\widetilde{f}
is
C^{1}
and strictly convex, so

\partial f(u)=\begin{cases}\widetilde{f}^{\prime}(u)&\text{if }u>0,\\
\left]-\infty,0\right]&\text{if }u=0,\\
\emptyset&\text{if }u<0\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\partial_{u}P(u,v)=\begin{cases}\widetilde{f}^{\prime}(u)-(v-\mathsf{a})&\text{if }u>0,\\
-v+\left]-\infty,\mathsf{a}\right]&\text{if }u=0,\\
\emptyset&\text{if }u<0.\end{cases}

The nonnegativity of
\rho_{\varepsilon}
suppresses the multi-valued part of
\partial_{u}P(\rho_{\varepsilon},\phi_{\varepsilon})
in the product
\rho_{\varepsilon}\partial_{u}P(\rho_{\varepsilon},\phi_{\varepsilon})
, which allows us to rewrite the approximate pressure as

(6.10)

\begin{gathered}\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}=\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\frac{\partial_{u}P(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})}{\varepsilon_{n}}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}P(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\\
+\Big(\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})}+\sqrt{f^{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\mathsf{a})}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}\Big)\Big(\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})}-\sqrt{f^{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\mathsf{a})}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}\Big).\end{gathered}

The last term on the r.h.s. of the first line is bounded over
n\in\mathbb{N}
(cf. (
6.8
)) it vanishes as
n\to\infty
(cf. (
6.9
)), so we only need to study the bottom line. We note
\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})
is nonnegative because
f
is convex and vanishes at
0
.

The aim of the next two steps is to show that
\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}
vanishes in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))
. We will accomplish this by showing, after weighting by
(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}
, that the first factor in the difference of squares is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
and that the second factor, without a weight, vanishes in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
.

Step 2:
(Weighted density estimate) Consider the function

\overline{W}(x,u)\coloneq f(u)-[\mathsf{c}(x)g]^{\ast}(u)+\mathsf{a}u=W(u)+[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(u)-[\mathsf{c}(x)g]^{\ast}(u)\geq 0,

where the inequality is a consequence of nonnegativity of
W
and the order-reversing property of the Legendre transform:
\mathsf{c}(x)g\geq{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g
implies
[\mathsf{c}(x)g]^{\ast}\leq[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}
. On the region
\Omega_{0}
, the function
\overline{W}(x,\cdot)
is a double-well potential since it clearly coincides with
W
. If
x\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{0}
, then
\overline{W}(x,u)>W(u)
for
u>0
and they coincide (vanish) only when
u=0
. We first show there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(6.11)

\overline{W}(x,u)\geq C(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})u^{m}\text{ for all }x\in\overline{\Omega}\text{ and }u\geq 0.

The coercivity of
W
(Lemma
3.1
-
(iii)
) provides the estimate
\overline{W}(x,u)\gtrsim u^{m}\gtrsim(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})u^{m}
for
u>R_{3}
, so it remains to obtain the estimate for
0\leq u\leq 1/R_{1}
and
1/R_{1}\leq u\leq R_{3}
.

Nonnegativity of
W
and an easy calculation using the first-order convexity inequality with
g^{\ast}
give for all
x\in\overline{\Omega}
and
u\geq 0
:

\overline{W}(x,u)\geq[{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\,g]^{\ast}(u)-[\mathsf{c}(x)g]^{\ast}(u)\geq(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})\Big[g^{\ast}\big(\frac{u}{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}\big)+(g^{\ast})^{\prime}\big(\frac{u}{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}\big)\frac{u}{{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}}\Big].

The term
