(g^{\ast})^{\prime}(u/{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})(u/{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})
in brackets is nonnegative. We estimate
g^{\ast}(\cdot/{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})
from below. By
(G2)
, we have for
0\leq u\leq{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}/R_{1}
that
g^{\ast}(u/{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})\gtrsim u^{m}
, which gives the estimate for small
u
. In the intermediate range
1/R_{1}\leq u\leq R_{3}
, since each of
u\mapsto g^{\ast}(u/{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})
and
u\mapsto u^{m}
is continuous and bounded away from zero, there exists a constant such that for all
1/R_{1}\leq u\leq R_{3}
we have
g^{\ast}(u/{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})\gtrsim u^{m}
. This proves (
6.11
).

Let
\overline{R}(x,u,v)
be defined in the same manner as
R(u,v)
(cf. (
6.7
)), but with
\mathsf{c}(x)
in place of

\textstyle\mathsf{c}
\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}

. Then,
\overline{R}\geq 0
, and we have for all
x\in\overline{\Omega}
,
u\geq 0
, and
v\in\mathbb{R}

\overline{W}(x,u)=W(u)+R(u,v)-\overline{R}(x,u,v)+(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(v)\leq W(u)+R(u,v)+(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(v).

After evaluating the above at
u=\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)
and
v=\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)
and integrating over
\Omega
, we find the right-hand side is bounded above by
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon_{n}}[\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t),\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)]
, so combining this with the estimate (
6.11
), the energy dissipation inequality, and the well-preparedness of the initial data yields the weighted density estimate

(6.12)

0\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)^{m}\,dx\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}\overline{W}(x,\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x))\,dx\leq\overline{\mathscr{G}}.

Step 3:
(Weighted approximate pressure convergence) The aim of this step is to show

(6.13)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}=0\text{ strongly in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega)).

We use the identity (
6.10
) derived in Step 1. For the first term, we combine
(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega)
with (
6.8
) to see that
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega))
-bound holds for
P(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}/\varepsilon_{n}
. Further, since
\int_{\Omega}P(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}/\varepsilon_{n}\,dx
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T)
and vanishes in
L^{1}(0,T)
, it vanishes, in particular, in
L^{2}(0,T)
. It remains to address the difference of squares in (
6.10
).

We examine the nonlinearity in the first factor. For all
u\geq 0
and
v\in\mathbb{R}

\Big(\sqrt{uf^{\prime}(u)-f(u)}+\sqrt{f^{\ast}(v-\mathsf{a})}\Big)^{2}\leq 2(uf^{\prime}(u)-f(u))+2f^{\ast}(v-\mathsf{a})\leq 2(uf^{\prime}(u)-f(u))+2g(v).

The definition of
\mathsf{a}
(cf.
(F&G)
) and the order-reversing property of the Legendre transform give
f^{\ast}(\cdot-\mathsf{a})\leq g
, which yields the last inequality. Since
f
is of porous medium-type, we have
uf^{\prime}(u)-f(u)=u^{m}
for all
u\geq 0
. Then (
6.12
) and equipartition of the energy (Lemma
5.1
-
(ii)
) give

(6.14)

\begin{gathered}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})}+\sqrt{f^{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\mathsf{a})}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\\
\lesssim\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t)^{m}\,dx+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})g(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t))\,dx\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\sqrt{\overline{\mathscr{G}}}.\end{gathered}

We show the second factor from the difference of squares in (
6.10
) vanishes in
L^{2}(\left]0,T\right[\times\Omega)
. We finally use the assumption that
f(u)=u^{m}/(m-1)+\iota_{\left[0,+\infty\right[}(u)
with
m>q^{\prime}
(recall
(F2)
), in which case
f^{\ast}(v)=(v/m^{\prime})_{+}^{m^{\prime}}
, where
(v)_{+}\coloneq\max\left\{v,0\right\}
. Then for this particular
f
, the function
P
from (
6.7
) is

P(u,v)=\frac{m^{\prime}}{m}u^{m}-u(v-\mathsf{a})+\big(\frac{v-\mathsf{a}}{m^{\prime}}\big)_{+}^{m^{\prime}}.

We claim
P
dominates the nonlinearity in the first factor for all
u\geq 0
and
v\in\mathbb{R}
:

N(u,v)\coloneq\big(\sqrt{uf^{\prime}(u)-f(u)}-\sqrt{f^{\ast}(v-\mathsf{a})}\big)^{2}=u^{m}-2u^{m/2}\big(\frac{v-\mathsf{a}}{m^{\prime}}\big)_{+}^{m^{\prime}/2}+\big(\frac{v-\mathsf{a}}{m^{\prime}}\big)_{+}^{m^{\prime}}\leq mP(u,v).

For clarity, we change variables
w\coloneq(v-\mathsf{a})/m^{\prime}
and, by an abuse of notation, write
P(u,w)=\frac{m^{\prime}}{m}u^{m}-m^{\prime}uw+w_{+}^{m^{\prime}}
and
N(u,w)=u^{m}-2u^{m/2}w^{m^{\prime}/2}_{+}+w_{+}^{m^{\prime}}
. The bound
N(u,w)\leq mP(u,w)
is clearly true for all
u\geq 0
and
w\leq 0
or
u=0
and
w>0
, so we show
mP(u,w)-N(u,w)\geq 0
for all
u,w>0
. Recall
m-1=m/m^{\prime}
, so

\displaystyle mP(u,w)-N(u,w)=(m^{\prime}-1)u^{m}-2u^{m/2}w^{m^{\prime}/2}+(m-1)w^{m^{\prime}}-mm^{\prime}uw

\displaystyle=u^{m/2}w^{m^{\prime}/2}\left[\frac{m^{\prime}u^{m/2}}{mw^{m^{\prime}/2}}+2+\frac{mw^{m^{\prime}/2}}{m^{\prime}u^{m/2}}-mm^{\prime}\frac{uw}{u^{m/2}w^{m^{\prime}/2}}\right].

Again, we change variables: let
A\coloneq m^{\prime}u^{m/2}
and
B\coloneq mw^{m^{\prime}/2}
, so the above reads

\displaystyle=\frac{AB}{m^{\prime}m}\left[\frac{A}{B}+2+\frac{B}{A}-AB\left(\frac{m^{\prime}}{A}\right)^{2/m^{\prime}}\left(\frac{m}{B}\right)^{2/m}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{AB}{m^{\prime}m}\left[\sqrt{\frac{A}{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{B}{A}}+\left(m^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{B}{A}}\right)^{1/m^{\prime}}\left(m\sqrt{\frac{A}{B}}\right)^{1/m}\right]\times\left[\sqrt{\frac{A}{B}}+\sqrt{\frac{B}{A}}-\left(m^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{B}{A}}\right)^{1/m^{\prime}}\left(m\sqrt{\frac{A}{B}}\right)^{1/m}\right].

On the second line, the first two factors are positive, and the last factor is nonnegative by Young’s inequality, which is easily seen after another change of variables:
C^{m^{\prime}}\coloneq m^{\prime}\sqrt{B/A}
and
D^{m}\coloneq m\sqrt{A/B}
.

Thus, (
6.9
) gives

(6.15)

\begin{gathered}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})}-\sqrt{f^{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\mathsf{a})}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\left\|P(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})\right\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}\right)^{1/2}=0.\end{gathered}

Then the identity (
6.10
) and Hölder’s inequality give

\displaystyle\left\|\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}\leq\left\|\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}P(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}},\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}})(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})}+\sqrt{f^{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\mathsf{a})}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}(\mathsf{c}-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})^{1/2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\left\|\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}f^{\prime}(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})}-\sqrt{f^{\ast}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\mathsf{a})}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{n}}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}},

so (
6.9
), (
6.14
), and (
6.15
) together prove (
6.13
).

Step 4:
(Uniform pressure bound)
The purpose of this step is to show, by duality,

(6.16)

\left\{p_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}\text{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X}^{\ast}),\quad\text{where }p_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\coloneq\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)-\sum_{k}(\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t))_{\Omega_{0}^{k}}\chi_{\Omega_{0}^{k}}(x),

The family
\left\{\Omega_{0}^{k}\right\}_{k}
is the connected components of
\Omega_{0}
, and for
0<s<1
from
(C1)
, the space

\mathcal{X}\coloneq\left\{\zeta\in C^{1,s}(\overline{\Omega})\,:\,\text{for all }k\,\,\zeta|_{\partial\Omega_{0}^{k}}=0\text{ and }(\zeta)_{\Omega_{0}^{k}}=0\right\}
,
which is a Banach subspace of
C^{1,s}(\overline{\Omega})
. By definition,
p_{\varepsilon_{n}}
is mean-zero on each
\Omega_{0}^{k}
.

Test vector fields
\xi
are obtained by solving potential problems. Given
\varphi\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X})
, we solve

\text{for a.e.\ }t\in[0,T]\qquad\begin{cases}\Delta u(t)=\varphi(t)&\text{in }\accentset{\circ}{\Omega}_{0}^{k}\text{ for each }k,\\
\nabla u(t)\cdot\vec{n}_{0}=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega_{0}^{k}\text{ for each }k,\end{cases}

which exists since
\varphi(t)
is mean-zero on each
\Omega_{0}^{k}
. We take
u(t)
to be the mean-zero solution, so Schauder estimates
[
Nar15
]
give
\left\|u(t)\right\|_{C^{3,s}(\Omega_{0})}\lesssim\left\|\varphi(t)\right\|_{C^{1,s}(\overline{\Omega})}
, and in particular
u(t)\in C^{2,1}(\Omega_{0})
, so we can extend
u(t)
to some
\widetilde{u}(t)\in C^{2,1}(\overline{\Omega})
such that each of
u(t)
,
\nabla u(t)
are compactly supported in
\Omega
and such that
\left\|\widetilde{u}(t)\right\|_{C^{2,1}(\overline{\Omega})}\lesssim\left\|u(t)\right\|_{C^{3,s-1}(\Omega_{0})}
. This may be achieved by first using a Whitney extension-type theorem
[
Ste71
, Chapter VI, Theorem 4]
,
[
Fef05
]
to extend
u(t)
from
\Omega_{0}
to
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and second multiplying that extension by a smooth cut-off function that is one on
\Omega_{0}
and zero on
\left\{x\in\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0}\,:\,\dist(x,\partial\Omega_{0})\geq\delta\right\}
, for some
\delta\in\left]0,\dist(\Omega_{0},\partial\Omega)\right[
. Only here do we use assumption
(C1)
that
(\partial\Omega_{0})\cap\partial\Omega=\emptyset
.

Let
\xi\coloneq\nabla\widetilde{u}
, which satisfies
\xi\cdot\vec{n}_{0}=0
on
[0,T]\times\partial\Omega_{0}
and
\xi=0
on
[0,T]\times\partial\Omega
, so in particular
\xi\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
[0,T]\times\partial\Omega
. We test (
6.6
) with this particular
\xi
, and then replace
\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}
by
p_{\varepsilon_{n}}
on the region
\Omega_{0}
, which is possible since
\xi
is divergence-free on each connected component of
\Omega_{0}
. This gives

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}p_{\varepsilon_{n}}\varphi\,dx\,dt\right|\leq\left|\alpha_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\big[f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}(\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\mathsf{a})\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0}}\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\Delta\widetilde{u}\,dx\,dt\right|.

We estimate each term on the right-hand side separately. Lemma
4.1
-
(ii)
and (
6.3
) of Lemma
6.2
combined with the boundedness of our extension of
u(t)
from
\Omega_{0}
to
\overline{\Omega}
and the Schauder estimate show the first integral is bounded by

\lesssim\left\|\xi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{1,1}(\overline{\Omega}))}\leq\left\|\widetilde{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{2,1}(\overline{\Omega}))}\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{2,1}(\Omega_{0}))}\leq\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{3,s}(\Omega_{0}))}\lesssim\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X})}.

The integral on the second line is also bounded by
\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X})}
, and it additionally vanishes as
n\to\infty
. Since
\Delta u(t)
is continuous up to
\partial\Omega_{0}
, we have
\Delta u(t)|_{\partial\Omega_{0}}=\varphi(t)|_{\partial\Omega_{0}}=0
from the definition of
\mathcal{X}
. By our choice of extension
\widetilde{u}(t)
,
\Delta\widetilde{u}(t)
is Lipschitz on
\overline{\Omega}
, which is why we chose
\mathcal{X}
to be composed of
C^{1,s}
functions. This provides

\text{for all }x\in\overline{\Omega}\setminus\Omega_{0}\quad\left|\Delta\widetilde{u}(t,x)\right|\leq\Lip(\Delta\widetilde{u}(t))\dist(x,\partial\Omega_{0})\lesssim\Lip(\Delta\widetilde{u}(t))\big(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\big)^{1/2}.

The last inequality is a consequence of
(C1)
, and we thus have the bound

(6.17)

\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0}}\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\Delta{\widetilde{u}}\,dx\,dt\right|\leq\int_{0}^{T}\Lip(\Delta\widetilde{u}(t))\left(\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0}}\left|\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\right|\big(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\big)^{1/2}\,dx\right)\,dt.

Combining Cauchy-Schwarz for the integral over
\left]0,T\right[
, the bound

\left\|\Lip(\Delta\widetilde{u})\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T)}\leq\left\|\widetilde{u}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{2,1}(\overline{\Omega}))}\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;C^{3,s}(\Omega_{0}))}\lesssim\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X})},

and (
6.13
) from Step 3 provides both the desired estimate

\text{for all }\varphi\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X})\quad\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}p_{\varepsilon_{n}}\varphi\,dx\,dt\right|\lesssim\left\|\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X})},

which implies (
6.16
), and that the right-hand side of (
6.17
) vanishes as
n\to\infty
.

Step 5:
(Passage to the limit) In (
6.6
), take
\xi\in L^{2}(0,T;C^{2,s}(\overline{\Omega}))
such that
\mathrm{div}\,\xi=\xi\cdot\vec{n}_{0}=0
on
[0,T]\times\partial\Omega_{0}
and
\xi\cdot\vec{n}=0
on
[0,T]\times\partial\Omega
, so
\mathrm{div}\,\xi\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X})
. For such
\xi
, we may then replace
\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}
in (
6.6
) by
p_{\varepsilon_{n}}
on the region
\Omega_{0}
, so

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega_{0}}p_{\varepsilon_{n}}\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx\,dt=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\vec{j}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{n}\alpha_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\big[f(\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}})+\mathsf{a}\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\big]\mathrm{div}\,\xi-\rho_{\varepsilon_{n}}\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\cdot\xi\,dx\,dt-\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{0}}\pi_{\varepsilon_{n}}\mathrm{div}\,\xi\,dx\,dt.

The result (
6.16
) of Step 4 provides
p\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X}^{\ast})
and a subsequence of
\left\{p_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right\}
such that
p_{\varepsilon_{n}}
converges to
p
weakly-
\ast
in
L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{X}^{\ast})
, which, after extraction, allows to pass to the limit in the first term. The convergence of the next two terms is handled by Proposition
4.2
-
(i)
and Proposition
6.1
, resp. The last term vanishes in the limit. To see this, one argues as in the derivation of (
6.17
), but with
\mathrm{div}\,\xi(t)
in place of
\Delta\widetilde{u}(t)
, and combines the analogous inequality to (
6.17
) with the weighted convergence (
6.13
).

Appendix A
A sufficient condition for nondegeneracy

The following is a sufficient condition for the nondegeneracy condition
(C1)
to be satisfied. We recall
(C1)
is used in the convergence of the partial first variation of
\mathscr{G}_{\varepsilon}
w.r.t. its first variable w.r.t. domain variations (Proposition
6.1
) as well as in obtaining a uniform bound on the approximate pressures.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
\mathsf{c}\in C^{2,1}(\overline{\Omega})
and set
{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\coloneq\min_{x\in\overline{\Omega}}\mathsf{c}(x)
. Suppose
\Omega_{0}\coloneq\left\{\mathsf{c}={\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\right\}
is a regular closed set, i.e.,
\overline{\Omega}_{0}=\overline{\accentset{\circ}{\Omega}_{0}}
, and
\partial\Omega_{0}
is
C^{2,1}
. If there exists a neighborhood
U
of
\partial\Omega_{0}
and a constant
\lambda>0
such that

\Delta\mathsf{c}(x)\geq\lambda>0\text{ for all }x\in U\setminus\accentset{\circ}{\Omega}_{0},

then

(i)

\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\geq\frac{\lambda}{2}\dist(x,\Omega_{0})^{2}
for all
x\in U
;

(ii)

there exists a constant
K>0
such that for all vector fields
\xi\in C^{1}(\overline{U};\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\xi\cdot\vec{n}_{0}=0
on
\partial\Omega_{0}
, where
\vec{n}_{0}
is the outward unit normal vector to
\partial\Omega_{0}
, we have for all
x\in U
the estimate
\left|\nabla\mathsf{c}(x)\cdot\xi(x)\right|\leq K(\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}})\left\|\xi\right\|_{C^{1}(\overline{U};\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

Proof.

Since
\partial\Omega_{0}
is compact, it suffices to show the estimates near a fixed
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega_{0}
and apply the resulting bounds to a finite open cover. After a possible translation and rotation of the coordinate axes, we can assume
x_{0}=0
and the outer unit normal vector to
\partial\Omega_{0}
at
0
is
\vec{n}_{0}=-\vec{e}_{d}=(0,0,\dots,-1)
. We write
\Omega_{0}
locally as an epigraph, so let
(\Theta,B)
be a
C^{2,1}
local parameterization of
\partial\Omega_{0}
around
0
. Then
x_{d}=\Theta(x^{\prime})
, where
x^{\prime}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d-1})
, satisfies
\Theta(0)=0
and
\nabla\Theta(0)=0
. The second-order Taylor polynomial of
\Theta
about
0
gives
\Theta(x^{\prime})=O(\left|x^{\prime}\right|^{2})
.

(i)
: Define

S(y)\coloneq\mathsf{c}(y^{\prime},\Theta(y^{\prime})+y_{d})-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\quad\text{ with }y=(y^{\prime},y_{d})\coloneq(x^{\prime},x_{d}-\Theta(x^{\prime})),

so
S(y)=0
when
y_{d}\geq 0
is small. Consequently,
\partial_{k}S(y^{\prime},0)=\partial_{j,k}^{2}S(y^{\prime},0)=0
for all
j\in\left\{1,\dots,d-1\right\}
,
k\in\left\{1,\dots,d\right\}
. Further,

\Delta_{x}\mathsf{c}(x)=\Delta_{y}S(y)+\sum_{j=1}^{d-1}\Big[\big(\left|\partial_{x_{j}}\Theta\right|^{2}-1\big)\partial_{y_{d},y_{j}}S-\partial_{x_{j}}\Theta\partial_{y_{j}}^{2}S-\partial_{y_{j}}S\partial_{x_{j}}^{2}\Theta\Big]_{y=(x^{\prime},x_{d}-\Theta(x^{\prime}))},

so the above implies that when
x_{d}=\Theta(x^{\prime})
we have
\partial_{y_{d}}^{2}S(y^{\prime},0)=\Delta_{x}\mathsf{c}(x)\geq\lambda
.

Define
h(s)\coloneq S(y^{\prime},sy_{d})
, so
h(0)=0
and the fundamental theorem of calculus give, for
y_{d}
small,

\displaystyle S(y)=h(1)=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{s}h^{\prime\prime}(t)\,dt\,ds=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{s}y_{d}^{2}\partial_{y_{d}}^{2}S(y^{\prime},ty_{d})\,dt\,ds=y_{d}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\partial_{y_{d}}^{2}S(y^{\prime},ty_{d})\,dt\geq\frac{\lambda}{2}y_{d}^{2}.

Changing variables shows

(A.1)

\mathsf{c}(x)-{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.22221pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-2.22221pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.55556pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-1.1111pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{c}$}\hskip-1.1111pt\hskip-2.5pt\raisebox{-5.42776pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\underline{\hphantom{\mathsf{c}}}$}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 2.5pt}}}\geq\frac{\lambda}{2}(x_{d}-\Theta(x^{\prime}))^{2}\geq\frac{\lambda}{2}\dist(x,\partial\Omega_{0})^{2},

which proves
(i)
.

(ii)
: A similar calculation using the fundamental theorem of calculus and
\mathsf{c},\Theta\in C^{2,1}
provides

(A.2a)

\displaystyle\forall\,j\in\left\{1,\dots,d-1\right\}\qquad\partial_{y_{j}}S(y)=y_{d}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\partial_{y_{d},y_{d},y_{j}}^{3}{S}(y^{\prime},ty_{d})\,dt=O(y_{d}^{2}),

(A.2b)

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{d}}S(y)=y_{d}\partial_{y_{d}}^{2}S(y^{\prime},0)+y_{d}^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\partial_{y_{d}}^{3}S(y^{\prime},ty_{d})\,dt=y_{d}\Delta\mathsf{c}(x)+O(y_{d}^{2}).

Writing
\xi^{\prime}(x)\coloneq(\xi^{1}(x),\dots,\xi^{d-1}(x))
,
\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\mathsf{c}(x)=\nabla_{y^{\prime}}S(y)-\partial_{y_{d}}S(y)\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\Theta(x^{\prime})
, and
\partial_{x_{d}}\mathsf{c}(x)=\partial_{y_{d}}S(y)
, we compute

\displaystyle\nabla\mathsf{c}(x)\cdot\xi(x)=\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\mathsf{c}(x)\cdot\xi^{\prime}(x)+\partial_{x_{d}}\mathsf{c}(x)\xi^{d}(x)=\nabla_{y^{\prime}}S(y)\cdot\xi^{\prime}(x)-\partial_{y_{d}}S(y)\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\Theta(x^{\prime})\cdot\xi^{\prime}(x)+\partial_{y_{d}}S(y)\xi^{d}(x).

We note that (
A.2a
) shows
\left|\nabla_{y^{\prime}}S(y)\right|=O(y_{d}^{2})
. Also, the outer unit normal
\vec{n}_{0}
to
\partial\Omega_{0}
is locally

\vec{n}_{0}(x)=(\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\Theta(x^{\prime}),-1)/\sqrt{\left|\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\Theta(x^{\prime})\right|^{2}+1},

so, by hypothesis,
0=\xi(x)\cdot\vec{n}_{0}(x)=\xi^{\prime}(x)\cdot\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\Theta(x^{\prime})-\xi^{d}(x)
on
\left\{x_{d}=\Theta(x^{\prime})\right\}
. These, together with Taylor’s theorem, give

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\mathsf{c}(x)\cdot\xi(x)\right|

\displaystyle=O(y_{d}^{2})\left\|\xi\right\|_{C(\overline{U})^{d}}-\partial_{y_{d}}S(y)\Big[\xi^{\prime}(x)\cdot\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\Theta(x^{\prime})-\xi^{d}(x)-\big(\xi^{\prime}(x^{\prime},\Theta(x^{\prime}))\cdot\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\Theta(x^{\prime})-\xi^{d}(x^{\prime},\Theta(x^{\prime}))\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=O(y_{d}^{2})\left\|\xi\right\|_{C(\overline{U})^{d}}+O(\left|y_{d}\right|)\times\left\|\nabla\xi\right\|_{C(\overline{U})^{d\times d}}O(\left|x_{d}-\Theta(x^{\prime})\right|)=O(y_{d}^{2})\left\|\xi\right\|_{C^{1}(\overline{U})^{d}}.

In view of (
A.1
), the claim is proven.
∎
